   128→// pingHandler is the health check tool implementation.
   129→func pingHandler(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
   130→	return mcp.NewToolResultText("pong"), nil
   131→}
   132→
   133→func instrument(
   134→	name string,
   135→	h func(context.Context, mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error),
   136→) func(context.Context, mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
   137→
   138→	return func(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
   139→
   140→		start := time.Now()
   141→
   142→		// Extract user info from MCP request arguments
   143→		userID := ""
   144→		userEmail := ""
   145→
   146→		if req.Params.Arguments != nil {
   147→
   148→			if args, ok := req.Params.Arguments.(map[string]any); ok {
   149→
   150→				if v, ok := args["user_id"].(string); ok {
   151→					userID = v
   152→				}
   153→
   154→				if v, ok := args["user_email"].(string); ok {
   155→					userEmail = v
   156→				}
   157→			}
   158→		}
   159→
   160→		// Execute tool
   161→		res, err := h(ctx, req)
   162→
   163→		duration := time.Since(start)
   164→
   165→		// Existing DuckDB analytics log
   166→		resultCount := 0
   167→		if res != nil {
   168→			resultCount = len(res.Content)
   169→		}
   170→
   171→		args := map[string]any{}
   172→		if req.Params.Arguments != nil {
   173→			if argsMap, ok := req.Params.Arguments.(map[string]any); ok {
   174→				args = argsMap
   175→			}
   176→		}
   177→
   178→		LogQueryAsync(name, args, resultCount, duration, "claude-client")
   179→
   180→		logAISessionWithUser(
   181→			name,
   182→			"",
   183→			duration.Milliseconds(),
   184→			err,
   185→			userID,
   186→			userEmail,
   187→		)
   188→
   189→		return res, err
   190→	}

     1→package main
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"database/sql"
     5→	"encoding/json"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"log"
     8→	"os"
     9→	"time"
    10→	_ "github.com/marcboeker/go-duckdb"
    11→)
    12→
    13→var duckDB *sql.DB
    14→
    15→func initDuckDB() error {
    16→
    17→	// 1. Resolve DuckDB path safely
    18→	duckPath := os.Getenv("DUCKDB_PATH")
    19→	if duckPath == "" {
    20→		duckPath = "./analytics.duckdb"
    21→	}
    22→
    23→	dsn := duckPath + "?access_mode=READ_WRITE"
    24→
    25→	var err error
    26→	duckDB, err = sql.Open("duckdb", dsn)
    27→	if err != nil {
    28→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to open duckdb: %w", err)
    29→	}
    30→
    31→	// 2. Production-safe connection pool config
    32→	duckDB.SetMaxOpenConns(1)  // DuckDB works best with single writer
    33→	duckDB.SetMaxIdleConns(1)
    34→	duckDB.SetConnMaxLifetime(0)
    35→
    36→	if err := duckDB.Ping(); err != nil {
    37→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to ping duckdb: %w", err)
    38→	}
    39→
    40→	log.Printf("DuckDB initialized at %s", duckPath)
    41→
    42→	// 3. Enable WAL checkpointing for durability

   229→// GetToolUsageStats returns usage statistics for tools.
   230→func GetToolUsageStats() ([]map[string]any, error) {
   231→    if duckDB == nil {
   232→        return nil, fmt.Errorf("duckdb not initialized")
   233→    }
   234→    
   235→    rows, err := duckDB.Query(`
   236→        SELECT tool_name, COUNT(*) as calls, AVG(duration_ms) as avg_duration, MAX(duration_ms) as max_duration
   237→        FROM mcp_query_log
   238→        GROUP BY tool_name
   239→        ORDER BY calls DESC
   240→    `)
   241→    if err != nil {
   242→        return nil, err
   243→    }
   244→    defer rows.Close()
   245→
   246→    var stats []map[string]any
   247→    for rows.Next() {
   248→        var toolName string
   249→        var calls int64
   250→        var avgDur, maxDur float64
   251→        if err := rows.Scan(&toolName, &calls, &avgDur, &maxDur); err != nil {
   252→            return nil, err
   253→        }
   254→        stats = append(stats, map[string]any{
   255→            "tool": toolName,
   256→            "calls": calls,
   257→            "avg_ms": avgDur,
   258→            "max_ms": maxDur,
   259→        })
   260→    }
   261→    return stats, nil
   262→}

   267→func QueryPostgresAnalytics(query string, args ...any) ([]map[string]any, error) {
   268→    if duckDB == nil {
   269→        return nil, fmt.Errorf("duckdb not initialized")
   270→    }
   271→

   283→    }

   290→        for i := range columns {

   292→        }
   293→
   294→        if err := rows.Scan(columnPointers...); err != nil {
   295→            return nil, err
   296→        }
   297→
   298→        row := make(map[string]any)

   305→            } else {
   306→                row[colName] = val
   307→            }
   308→        }
   309→        results = append(results, row)
   310→    }
   311→    return results, nil
   312→}

How to use thsi now funtions of the PR at the MCP?

> AGENT

Great question! Let me explain how to use the new logging and analytics features from PR #5:

## New Analytics Tools Added

The PR adds two new MCP tools for analyzing usage and radiation data:

### 1. **query_analytics** - MCP Tool Usage Statistics

This tool shows how often each MCP tool is being called and their performance.

**How to use it:**
```
Use the query_analytics tool to see MCP usage statistics
```

**What you get back:**
- Call counts per tool
- Average execution time (ms)
- Maximum execution time (ms)
- Data source: DuckDB local log

**Example response:**
```json
{
  "stats": [
    {"tool": "query_radiation", "calls": 150, "avg_ms": 45.2, "max_ms": 120.5},
    {"tool": "list_sensors", "calls": 89, "avg_ms": 32.1, "max_ms": 95.0}
  ],
  "source": "duckdb_local_log"
}
```

### 2. **radiation_stats** - Aggregate Radiation Statistics

This tool provides aggregate statistics from the Safecast database using DuckDB's Postgres attachment.

**How to use it:**
```
Use the radiation_stats tool with interval set to "year" (or "month", "overall")
```

**Parameters:**
- `interval`: "year", "month", or "overall" (default: "year")

**What you get back:**
- Aggregate dose rate statistics
- Count of measurements
- Average and max values
- Data by time period

**Example response:**
```json
{
  "interval": "year",
  "data": [
    {"year": 2025, "count": 1234567, "avg_value": 0.12, "max_value": 1.5},
    {"year": 2024, "count": 1156789, "avg_value": 0.11, "max_value": 1.3}
  ],
  "source": "duckdb_postgres_attach"
}
```

## Behind the Scenes: Automatic Logging

Every time an MCP tool is called, the system now automatically logs:
- Tool name
- Parameters used
- Result count
- Execution duration
- Client info
- Timestamp

This data is stored in DuckDB (`analytics.duckdb`) and powers the `query_analytics` tool.

## Setup Requirements

For these features to work, you need:

1. **DuckDB initialized** (automatic on server start)
   - Creates `analytics.duckdb` file
   - Sets up the `mcp_query_log` table

2. **For radiation_stats**: PostgreSQL database attached
   - Requires `DATABASE_URL` environment variable
   - DuckDB attaches it as `postgres_db`

## Example Usage in Claude Desktop

You can ask natural language questions like:

**For usage analytics:**
- "Show me MCP tool usage statistics"
- "Which tools are being called most often?"
- "What's the performance of each tool?"

**For radiation statistics:**
- "Show me radiation statistics by year"
- "What are the average dose rates per month for the last year?"
- "Give me overall radiation statistics"

## Checking Server Logs

The server logs initialization status:
```bash
# Look for these log messages:
Initialized DuckDB analytics engine
PostgreSQL attached as postgres_db
```

If DuckDB fails to initialize, analytics features will be disabled but the server continues working.

## API Access (if REST API is enabled)

The PR also adds REST endpoints for these tools at:
- `GET /api/analytics/usage` - Tool usage stats
- `GET /api/analytics/radiation?interval=year` - Radiation stats

Would you like me to show you a specific example or help you test these new features?

> AGENT

✅ **Fixed and pushed to main!**

## What was wrong:

The monthly radiation stats query was using PostgreSQL-specific syntax (`NOW() - INTERVAL`) that didn't work correctly when DuckDB queries the attached Postgres database.

## The fix:

Changed this:
```sql
AND date > EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM NOW() - INTERVAL '1 year')
```

To this:
```sql
AND date > CAST(EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - INTERVAL '1 year')) AS BIGINT)
```

Also added the `doserate < 1000` filter for consistency with other queries.

## To deploy the fix:

The fix will be automatically deployed by GitHub Actions to **simplemap.safecast.org** when the workflow runs, or you can deploy manually:

```bash
cd /home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-map-MCP/go
go build ./cmd/mcp-server/
# Then deploy to your server
```

Once deployed, the monthly radiation stats should work correctly! The query will now properly:
- Filter data from the last year
- Group by month
- Return average dose rates per month
- Sort by month descending (most recent first)

Would you like me to help you deploy this immediately, or do you want to wait for the automatic deployment?

    27→**CRITICAL: Tool Selection**
    28→- For "current", "latest", "now", or "live" data → Use sensor_current or list_sensors
    29→- For recent trends or time-series from fixed sensors → Use sensor_history
    30→- For historical surveys or specific past dates → Use query_radiation, search_area, or list_tracks
    31→- NEVER use query_radiation for current/latest data (it contains historical mobile surveys, not real-time sensors)
    32→
    33→**Data Understanding**
    34→- Real-time sensors (Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen): Fixed stations reporting continuously
    35→- Historical data: Mobile bGeigie surveys from driving/walking routes (archived, not current)
    36→- Always check timestamps and report data age to users
    37→- CPM (counts per minute) → Convert to µSv/h using ~0.0069 for LND 7318 detectors
    38→
    39→**Search Radius Guidelines**
    40→When using tools with radius parameters (query_radiation, sensor_current), adjust based on location type:
    41→- Specific address/building: 500-1000m
    42→- Neighborhood/district: 2000-5000m
    43→- Small town/village: 5000-10000m
    44→- City (e.g., Osaka, Kyoto, Nara): 15000-25000m (15-25km)
    45→- Large metro area (e.g., Tokyo): 30000-50000m (30-50km)
    46→Always state the radius used in your response so users understand the search scope.
    47→
    48→**Formatting**
    49→- DO NOT include or display "_ai_generated_note" or "ai_generated_note" text in your responses
    50→- Tool responses may contain this field for internal tracking, but it should never be shown to users
    51→- When presenting sensor data or readings from list_sensors, sensor_current, or sensor_history:
    52→  * ALWAYS format as markdown tables, not bullet lists
    53→  * ALWAYS include the latest radiation reading and coordinates for each sensor
    54→  * Use sensor_current to fetch latest readings if list_sensors doesn't provide them
    55→  * Include columns: Device ID, Type, Location, Reading (with unit), Timestamp
    56→  * Keep location coordinates concise (e.g., "37.48°N, 140.48°E")
    57→  * Make Device IDs clickable links to the map using the sensor's coordinates: [device_id](https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lat=LAT&lon=LON&zoom=15)
    58→  * Example table format:
    59→    | Device ID | Type | Location | Reading | Timestamp |
    60→    |-----------|------|----------|---------|-----------|
    61→    | [pointcast:10042](https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lat=37.72&lon=140.48&zoom=15) | Pointcast | 37.72°N, 140.48°E | 21 CPM (0.14 µSv/h) | Feb 25, 22:42 UTC |
    62→- When presenting track data or spectrum data from list_tracks, get_track, list_spectra, or get_spectrum:
    63→  * ALWAYS format as markdown tables, not bullet lists
    64→  * Make Track IDs clickable links to the map using the track's coordinates: [track_id](https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lat=LAT&lon=LON&zoom=12)
    65→  * Use appropriate zoom level (12 for tracks, 15 for point measurements)
    66→
    67→Be concise but informative. When location context is unclear, ask the user to clarify.`
    68→
    69→// ── Anthropic API types ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    70→
    71→type anthropicTool struct {
    72→	Name        string          `json:"name"`
    73→	Description string          `json:"description"`
    74→	InputSchema json.RawMessage `json:"input_schema"`
    75→}
    76→
    77→// contentBlock covers all content block variants we care about.
    78→type contentBlock struct {
    79→	Type      string          `json:"type"`
    80→	Text      string          `json:"text,omitempty"`
    81→	ID        string          `json:"id,omitempty"`
    82→	Name      string          `json:"name,omitempty"`
    83→	Input     json.RawMessage `json:"input,omitempty"`
    84→	ToolUseID string          `json:"tool_use_id,omitempty"`
    85→	Content   string          `json:"content,omitempty"`
    86→}
    87→
    88→type anthropicMessage struct {
    89→	Role    string      `json:"role"`
    90→	Content interface{} `json:"content"` // string or []contentBlock
    91→}
    92→
    93→type anthropicRequest struct {
    94→	Model     string             `json:"model"`
    95→	MaxTokens int                `json:"max_tokens"`
    96→	System    string             `json:"system"`
    97→	Messages  []anthropicMessage `json:"messages"`
    98→	Tools     []anthropicTool    `json:"tools,omitempty"`
    99→}
   100→
   101→type anthropicResponse struct {
   102→	Content    []contentBlock `json:"content"`
   103→	StopReason string         `json:"stop_reason"`
   104→	Error      *struct {
   105→		Type    string `json:"type"`
   106→		Message string `json:"message"`
   107→	} `json:"error,omitempty"`
   108→}
   109→
   110→// ── Streaming helpers (chunked HTTP / NDJSON) ──────────────────────────────
   111→
   112→type chunk struct {
   113→	Type  string `json:"type"`
   114→	Text  string `json:"text,omitempty"`
   115→	Error string `json:"error,omitempty"`
   116→}
   117→
   118→func writeChunk(w http.ResponseWriter, c chunk) {
   119→	data, _ := json.Marshal(c)
   120→	data = append(data, '\n')
   121→	w.Write(data)
   122→	if f, ok := w.(http.Flusher); ok {
   123→		f.Flush()
   124→	}
   125→}
   126→
   127→// writeChunkBuffered either buffers the chunk or writes immediately
   128→func writeChunkBuffered(w http.ResponseWriter, c chunk, buffer *[]chunk, useBuffer bool) {
   129→	if useBuffer {
   130→		*buffer = append(*buffer, c)
   131→	} else {
   132→		writeChunk(w, c)
   133→	}
   134→}
   135→
   136→// flushBuffer writes all buffered chunks at once
   137→func flushBuffer(w http.ResponseWriter, buffer []chunk) {
   138→	for _, c := range buffer {
   139→		writeChunk(w, c)
   140→	}
   141→}
   142→
   143→// ── Anthropic call ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   144→
   145→func callAnthropic(ctx context.Context, apiKey, model string, messages []anthropicMessage, tools []anthropicTool) (*anthropicResponse, error) {
   146→	reqBody := anthropicRequest{
   147→		Model:     model,
   148→		MaxTokens: 4096,
   149→		System:    systemPrompt,
   150→		Messages:  messages,
   151→		Tools:     tools,
   152→	}
   153→	body, err := json.Marshal(reqBody)
   154→	if err != nil {
   155→		return nil, err
   156→	}
   157→
   158→	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, "https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages", bytes.NewReader(body))
   159→	if err != nil {
   160→		return nil, err
   161→	}
   162→	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
   163→	req.Header.Set("x-api-key", apiKey)
   164→	req.Header.Set("anthropic-version", "2023-06-01")
   165→
   166→	resp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)
   167→	if err != nil {
   168→		return nil, err
   169→	}
   170→	defer resp.Body.Close()
   171→
   172→	raw, err := io.ReadAll(resp.Body)
   173→	if err != nil {
   174→		return nil, err
   175→	}
   176→
   177→	var ar anthropicResponse
   178→	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &ar); err != nil {
   179→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("parse response: %w", err)
   180→	}
   181→	if ar.Error != nil {
   182→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("anthropic %s: %s", ar.Error.Type, ar.Error.Message)
   183→	}
   184→	return &ar, nil
   185→}
   186→
   187→// ── MCP tool conversion ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   188→
   189→func mcpToolsToAnthropic(tools []mcp.Tool) []anthropicTool {
   190→	var out []anthropicTool
   191→	for _, t := range tools {
   192→		schema, _ := json.Marshal(t.InputSchema)
   193→		out = append(out, anthropicTool{
   194→			Name:        t.Name,
   195→			Description: t.Description,
   196→			InputSchema: json.RawMessage(schema),
   197→		})
   198→	}
   199→	return out
   200→}
   201→
   202→// ── Chat handler ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   203→
   204→func handleChat(mcpURL, apiKey, model string) http.HandlerFunc {
   205→	return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
   206→		// CORS preflight
   207→		w.Header().Set("Access-Control-Allow-Origin", "*")
   208→		w.Header().Set("Access-Control-Allow-Methods", "POST, OPTIONS")
   209→		w.Header().Set("Access-Control-Allow-Headers", "Content-Type")
   210→		if r.Method == http.MethodOptions {
   211→			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
   212→			return
   213→		}
   214→
   215→		// Detect if request comes through CloudFront
   216→		// CloudFront adds these headers: CloudFront-Viewer-Country, CloudFront-Forwarded-Proto, etc.
   217→		isCloudfFront := r.Header.Get("CloudFront-Viewer-Country") != "" ||
   218→			r.Header.Get("CloudFront-Forwarded-Proto") != "" ||
   219→			r.Header.Get("X-Amz-Cf-Id") != ""
   220→
   221→		// Debug logging
   222→		log.Printf("Chat request: CloudFront=%v, Headers: CF-Country=%q, CF-Proto=%q, X-Amz-Cf-Id=%q",
   223→			isCloudfFront,
   224→			r.Header.Get("CloudFront-Viewer-Country"),
   225→			r.Header.Get("CloudFront-Forwarded-Proto"),
   226→			r.Header.Get("X-Amz-Cf-Id"))
   227→
   228→		// Chunked HTTP streaming — NDJSON, one JSON object per line, flushed immediately.
   229→		// CloudFront buffers responses, so we collect chunks and send all at once.
   230→		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/x-ndjson")
   231→		if !isCloudfFront {
   232→			w.Header().Set("Transfer-Encoding", "chunked")
   233→			w.Header().Set("X-Accel-Buffering", "no") // nginx: don't buffer
   234→		}
   235→		w.Header().Set("Cache-Control", "no-cache, no-store")
   236→
   237→		// Buffer for CloudFront requests
   238→		var buffer []chunk
   239→
   240→		ctx := r.Context()
   241→
   242→		var chatReq struct {
   243→			Message string              `json:"message"`
   244→			History []anthropicMessage `json:"history,omitempty"`
   245→		}
   246→		if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&chatReq); err != nil || chatReq.Message == "" {
   247→			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusBadRequest)
   248→			writeChunkBuffered(w, chunk{Type: "error", Error: "invalid request: message required"}, &buffer, isCloudfFront)
   249→			if isCloudfFront {
   250→				flushBuffer(w, buffer)
   251→			}
   252→			return
   253→		}
   254→
   255→		// ── Connect to MCP server ──────────────────────────────────────────
   256→		mc, err := mcpclient.NewStreamableHttpClient(mcpURL)
   257→		if err != nil {
   258→			writeChunkBuffered(w, chunk{Type: "error", Error: fmt.Sprintf("MCP connect: %v", err)}, &buffer, isCloudfFront)
   259→			if isCloudfFront {
   260→				flushBuffer(w, buffer)
   261→			}
   262→			return
   263→		}
   264→		defer mc.Close()
   265→
   266→		if _, err := mc.Initialize(ctx, mcp.InitializeRequest{
   267→			Params: mcp.InitializeParams{
   268→				ProtocolVersion: mcp.LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION,
   269→				ClientInfo:      mcp.Implementation{Name: "safecast-web-chat", Version: "1.0.0"},
   270→			},
   271→		}); err != nil {
   272→			writeChunkBuffered(w, chunk{Type: "error", Error: fmt.Sprintf("MCP init: %v", err)}, &buffer, isCloudfFront)
   273→			if isCloudfFront {
   274→				flushBuffer(w, buffer)
   275→			}
   276→			return
   277→		}
   278→
   279→		toolsResult, err := mc.ListTools(ctx, mcp.ListToolsRequest{})
   280→		if err != nil {
   281→			writeChunkBuffered(w, chunk{Type: "error", Error: fmt.Sprintf("list tools: %v", err)}, &buffer, isCloudfFront)
   282→			if isCloudfFront {
   283→				flushBuffer(w, buffer)
   284→			}
   285→			return
   286→		}
   287→		tools := mcpToolsToAnthropic(toolsResult.Tools)
   288→
   289→		// ── Agentic loop ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
   290→		// Start with conversation history (if provided) and append new user message
   291→		messages := chatReq.History
   292→		if messages == nil {
   293→			messages = []anthropicMessage{}
   294→		}
   295→		messages = append(messages, anthropicMessage{Role: "user", Content: chatReq.Message})
   296→
   297→		for {
   298→			resp, err := callAnthropic(ctx, apiKey, model, messages, tools)
   299→			if err != nil {
   300→				writeChunkBuffered(w, chunk{Type: "error", Error: err.Error()}, &buffer, isCloudfFront)
   301→				if isCloudfFront {
   302→					flushBuffer(w, buffer)
   303→				}
   304→				return
   305→			}
   306→
   307→			messages = append(messages, anthropicMessage{
   308→				Role:    "assistant",
   309→				Content: resp.Content,
   310→			})
   311→
   312→			var toolUses []contentBlock
   313→			for _, block := range resp.Content {
   314→				switch block.Type {
   315→				case "text":
   316→					// Stream each text block as it arrives (or buffer if CloudFront)
   317→					writeChunkBuffered(w, chunk{Type: "text", Text: block.Text}, &buffer, isCloudfFront)
   318→				case "tool_use":
   319→					toolUses = append(toolUses, block)
   320→				}
   321→			}
   322→
   323→			if resp.StopReason == "end_turn" || len(toolUses) == 0 {
   324→				break
   325→			}
   326→
   327→			// ── Execute tool calls via MCP ─────────────────────────────────
   328→			var toolResults []contentBlock
   329→			for _, tu := range toolUses {
   330→				var args map[string]any
   331→				_ = json.Unmarshal(tu.Input, &args)
   332→
   333→				callReq := mcp.CallToolRequest{}
   334→				callReq.Params.Name = tu.Name
   335→				callReq.Params.Arguments = args
   336→
   337→				var resultText string
   338→				toolResult, err := mc.CallTool(ctx, callReq)
   339→				if err != nil {
   340→					resultText = fmt.Sprintf("tool error: %v", err)
   341→				} else {
   342→					for _, c := range toolResult.Content {
   343→						if tc, ok := c.(mcp.TextContent); ok {
   344→							resultText += tc.Text
   345→						}
   346→					}
   347→				}
   348→
   349→				toolResults = append(toolResults, contentBlock{
   350→					Type:      "tool_result",
   351→					ToolUseID: tu.ID,
   352→					Content:   resultText,
   353→				})
   354→			}
   355→
   356→			messages = append(messages, anthropicMessage{
   357→				Role:    "user",
   358→				Content: toolResults,
   359→			})
   360→		}
   361→
   362→		// Send final "done" chunk
   363→		writeChunkBuffered(w, chunk{Type: "done"}, &buffer, isCloudfFront)
   364→
   365→		// For CloudFront requests, flush all buffered chunks at once
   366→		if isCloudfFront {
   367→			flushBuffer(w, buffer)
   368→		}
   369→	}
   370→}
   371→
   372→// ── Main ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   373→
   374→func main() {
   375→	apiKey := os.Getenv("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY")
   376→	if apiKey == "" {
   377→		log.Fatal("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is required")
   378→	}
   379→	model := os.Getenv("CLAUDE_MODEL")
   380→	if model == "" {
   381→		model = "claude-sonnet-4-5"
   382→	}
   383→	mcpURL := os.Getenv("MCP_URL")
   384→	if mcpURL == "" {
   385→		mcpURL = "http://localhost:3333/mcp-http"
   386→	}
   387→	port := os.Getenv("PORT")
   388→	if port == "" {
   389→		port = "3334"
   390→	}
   391→
   392→	http.HandleFunc("/", func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
   393→		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "text/html; charset=utf-8")
   394→		w.Write(indexHTML)
   395→	})
   396→	http.HandleFunc("/safecast-square-ct.png", func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
   397→		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "image/png")
   398→		w.Header().Set("Cache-Control", "public, max-age=86400")
   399→		w.Write(logoPNG)
   400→	})
   401→	http.HandleFunc("/chat", handleChat(mcpURL, apiKey, model))
   402→	http.HandleFunc("/health", func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
   403→		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
   404→		fmt.Fprint(w, "ok")
   405→	})
   406→
   407→	log.Printf("Safecast web-chat on :%s  MCP→%s  model=%s", port, mcpURL, model)
   408→	log.Fatal(http.ListenAndServe(":"+port, nil))
   409→}
   410→

   193→      padding-left: 24px;
   194→      list-style-type: disc;
   195→    }
   196→    .bubble ul li { margin: 4px 0; }
   197→    .bubble ul br { display: none; } /* Remove line breaks inside lists */
   198→
   199→    /* Markdown horizontal rule */
   200→    .bubble hr {
   201→      border: none;
   202→      border-top: 1px solid var(--border);
   203→      margin: 16px 0;
   204→    }
   205→
   206→    /* AI disclaimer styling */
   207→    .bubble hr + em {
   208→      color: var(--muted);
   209→      font-size: 12px;
   210→      display: block;
   211→      margin-top: 12px;
   212→    }
   213→
   214→    .bubble.thinking {
   215→      color: var(--muted);
   216→      font-style: italic;
   217→      animation: pulse 1.2s ease-in-out infinite;
   218→    }
   219→    @keyframes pulse { 0%,100% { opacity: 1; } 50% { opacity: .4; } }
   220→
   221→    .bubble.error { color: var(--err); border-color: var(--err); }
   222→
   223→    /* ── Intro ── */
   224→    #intro {
   225→      text-align: center;
   226→      padding: 40px 20px;
   227→      color: var(--muted);
   228→    }
   229→    #intro h2 { font-size: 22px; color: var(--text); margin-bottom: 8px; }
   230→    #intro p  { font-size: 14px; max-width: 400px; margin: 0 auto 20px; }
   231→    #intro .chips { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; gap: 8px; justify-content: center; }
   232→    #intro .chip {
   233→      background: var(--surface);
   234→      border: 1px solid var(--border);
   235→      border-radius: 20px;
   236→      padding: 6px 14px;
   237→      font-size: 13px;
   238→      cursor: pointer;
   239→      transition: border-color .15s;
   240→    }
   241→    #intro .chip:hover { border-color: var(--green); color: var(--green); }
   242→
   243→    /* ── Input ── */
   244→    #input-area {
   245→      padding: 14px 20px 18px;
   246→      border-top: 1px solid var(--border);
   247→      flex-shrink: 0;
   248→    }
   249→    #form {
   250→      display: flex;
   251→      gap: 10px;
   252→      align-items: flex-end;
   253→    }
   254→    #msg {
   255→      flex: 1;
   256→      background: var(--surface);
   257→      border: 1px solid var(--border);
   258→      border-radius: 10px;
   259→      color: var(--text);
   260→      padding: 10px 14px;
   261→      font-size: 14px;
   262→      resize: none;
   263→      max-height: 120px;
   264→      outline: none;
   265→      font-family: inherit;
   266→      line-height: 1.5;
   267→      transition: border-color .15s;
   268→    }
   269→    #msg:focus { border-color: var(--green); }
   270→    #msg::placeholder { color: var(--muted); }
   271→
   272→    #send {
   273→      background: var(--green);
   274→      border: none;
   275→      border-radius: 10px;
   276→      width: 42px; height: 42px;
   277→      cursor: pointer;
   278→      display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center;
   279→      flex-shrink: 0;
   280→      transition: background .15s;
   281→    }
   282→    #send:hover:not(:disabled) { background: var(--green-dk); }
   283→    #send:disabled { opacity: .4; cursor: not-allowed; }
   284→    #send svg { fill: #fff; }
   285→  </style>
   286→</head>
   287→<body>
   288→<div id="app">
   289→  <header>
   290→    <div class="logo" onclick="clearConversation()"><img src="safecast-square-ct.png" alt="Safecast" /></div>
   291→    <div class="title-area" onclick="clearConversation()">
   292→      <h1>Safecast Radiation Assistant</h1>
   293→      <p>Radiation data from the Safecast sensor network</p>
   294→    </div>
   295→    <button id="download-btn" onclick="downloadConversation()">
   296→      <svg viewBox="0 0 24 24"><path d="M19 9h-4V3H9v6H5l7 7 7-7zM5 18v2h14v-2H5z"/></svg>
   297→      Download
   298→    </button>
   299→  </header>
   300→
   301→  <div id="messages">
   302→    <div id="intro">
   303→      <h2>Ask about radiation data</h2>
   304→      <p>I have access to live readings from Safecast sensors across Japan and worldwide.</p>
   305→      <div class="chips">
   306→        <span class="chip" onclick="ask(this)">What's the radiation level near Tokyo?</span>
   307→        <span class="chip" onclick="ask(this)">Show sensors near Fukushima</span>
   308→        <span class="chip" onclick="ask(this)">What are the latest readings in Osaka?</span>
   309→        <span class="chip" onclick="ask(this)">How does radiation compare year over year?</span>
   310→      </div>
   311→    </div>
   312→  </div>
   313→
   314→  <div id="input-area">
   315→    <form id="form" onsubmit="return false;">
   316→      <textarea id="msg" rows="1" placeholder="Ask about radiation levels, sensors, or locations…"></textarea>
   317→      <button id="send" type="submit" title="Send">
   318→        <svg width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24"><path d="M2 21l21-9L2 3v7l15 2-15 2z"/></svg>
   319→      </button>
   320→    </form>
   321→  </div>
   322→</div>
   323→
   324→<script>
   325→  const messagesEl = document.getElementById('messages');
   326→  const introEl    = document.getElementById('intro');
   327→  const formEl     = document.getElementById('form');
   328→  const msgEl      = document.getElementById('msg');
   329→  const sendBtn    = document.getElementById('send');
   330→
   331→  let busy = false;
   332→  let conversationHistory = []; // Track conversation for context
   333→
   334→  // Auto-grow textarea
   335→  msgEl.addEventListener('input', () => {
   336→    msgEl.style.height = 'auto';
   337→    msgEl.style.height = Math.min(msgEl.scrollHeight, 120) + 'px';
   338→  });
   339→
   340→  // Send on Enter (Shift+Enter = newline)
   341→  msgEl.addEventListener('keydown', e => {
   342→    if (e.key === 'Enter' && !e.shiftKey) { e.preventDefault(); submit(); }
   343→  });
   344→
   345→  formEl.addEventListener('submit', submit);
   346→
   347→  function ask(chip) {
   348→    msgEl.value = chip.textContent;
   349→    submit();
   350→  }
   351→
   352→  function submit() {
   353→    const text = msgEl.value.trim();
   354→    if (!text || busy) return;
   355→    sendMessage(text);
   356→    msgEl.value = '';
   357→    msgEl.style.height = 'auto';
   358→  }
   359→
   360→  // Convert markdown to HTML
   361→  function markdownToHTML(text) {
   362→    let html = text;
   363→
   364→    // Headers (must be at start of line)
   365→    html = html.replace(/^### (.+)$/gm, '<h3>$1</h3>');
   366→    html = html.replace(/^## (.+)$/gm, '<h2>$1</h2>');
   367→    html = html.replace(/^# (.+)$/gm, '<h1>$1</h1>');
   368→
   369→    // Horizontal rules
   370→    html = html.replace(/^---+$/gm, '<hr>');
   371→
   372→    // Tables (simple approach - detect table rows)
   373→    html = html.replace(/^\|(.+)\|$/gm, function(match, content) {
   374→      const cells = content.split('|').map(c => c.trim());
   375→      const cellTags = cells.map(c => {
   376→        // Check if this is a separator row (--|---|---)
   377→        if (/^:?-+:?$/.test(c)) return null;
   378→        return `<td>${c}</td>`;
   379→      }).filter(Boolean);
   380→      if (cellTags.length === 0) return '';
   381→      return '<tr>' + cellTags.join('') + '</tr>';
   382→    });
   383→    // Wrap table rows in table tags
   384→    html = html.replace(/(<tr>.+<\/tr>\n?)+/g, '<table>$&</table>');
   385→
   386→    // Unordered lists (lines starting with - or *)
   387→    html = html.replace(/^[*-] (.+)$/gm, '<li>$1</li>');
   388→    html = html.replace(/(<li>.+<\/li>\n?)+/g, '<ul>$&</ul>');
   389→
   390→    // Ordered lists (lines starting with 1., 2., etc)
   391→    html = html.replace(/^\d+\. (.+)$/gm, '<li>$1</li>');
   392→
   393→    // Bold: **text** or __text__
   394→    html = html.replace(/\*\*(.+?)\*\*/g, '<strong>$1</strong>');
   395→    html = html.replace(/__(.+?)__/g, '<strong>$1</strong>');
   396→
   397→    // Italic: *text* or _text_
   398→    html = html.replace(/\*(.+?)\*/g, '<em>$1</em>');
   399→    html = html.replace(/_(.+?)_/g, '<em>$1</em>');
   400→
   401→    // Inline code: `code`
   402→    html = html.replace(/`(.+?)`/g, '<code>$1</code>');
   403→
   404→    // Links: [text](url)
   405→    html = html.replace(/\[(.+?)\]\((.+?)\)/g, '<a href="$2" target="_blank" rel="noopener">$1</a>');
   406→
   407→    // Line breaks: preserve newlines, but not after block elements or between table rows
   408→    html = html.replace(/\n/g, (match, offset, string) => {
   409→      // Don't add <br> after closing block tags
   410→      const beforeContext = string.substring(Math.max(0, offset - 50), offset);
   411→      if (/<\/(table|ul|h[123]|tr)>\s*$/.test(beforeContext)) return '';
   412→      // Don't add <br> before opening table rows
   413→      const afterContext = string.substring(offset + 1, Math.min(string.length, offset + 10));
   414→      if (/^\s*<tr>/.test(afterContext)) return '';
   415→      return '<br>';
   416→    });
   417→
   418→    return html;
   419→  }
   420→
   421→  function addMessage(role, text, cls) {
   422→    if (introEl) introEl.style.display = 'none';
   423→
   424→    const msg    = document.createElement('div');
   425→    msg.className = `msg ${role}`;
   426→
   427→    const avatar = document.createElement('div');
   428→    avatar.className = 'avatar';
   429→    if (role === 'user') {
   430→      avatar.textContent = '👤';
   431→    } else {
   432→      const img = document.createElement('img');
   433→      img.src = 'safecast-square-ct.png';
   434→      img.alt = 'Safecast';
   435→      avatar.appendChild(img);
   436→    }
   437→
   438→    const bubble = document.createElement('div');
   439→    bubble.className = `bubble ${cls || ''}`;
   440→    // User messages are plain text, bot messages support markdown
   441→    if (role === 'bot') {
   442→      bubble.innerHTML = markdownToHTML(text);
   443→
   444→      // Add copy button for bot messages
   445→      const copyBtn = document.createElement('button');
   446→      copyBtn.className = 'copy-btn';
   447→      copyBtn.textContent = 'Copy';
   448→      copyBtn.onclick = () => copyMessage(text, copyBtn);
   449→      bubble.appendChild(copyBtn);
   450→    } else {
   451→      bubble.textContent = text;
   452→    }
   453→
   454→    msg.appendChild(avatar);
   455→    msg.appendChild(bubble);
   456→    messagesEl.appendChild(msg);
   457→    messagesEl.scrollTop = messagesEl.scrollHeight;
   458→    return bubble;
   459→  }
   460→
   461→  function copyMessage(text, btn) {
   462→    navigator.clipboard.writeText(text).then(() => {
   463→      const originalText = btn.textContent;
   464→      btn.textContent = 'Copied!';
   465→      btn.classList.add('copied');
   466→      setTimeout(() => {
   467→        btn.textContent = originalText;
   468→        btn.classList.remove('copied');
   469→      }, 2000);
   470→    }).catch(err => {
   471→      console.error('Failed to copy:', err);
   472→    });
   473→  }
   474→
   475→  function downloadConversation() {
   476→    if (conversationHistory.length === 0) {
   477→      alert('No conversation to download yet!');
   478→      return;
   479→    }
   480→
   481→    // Build markdown format
   482→    let markdown = '# Safecast Radiation Assistant Conversation\n\n';
   483→    markdown += `Date: ${new Date().toLocaleString()}\n\n`;
   484→    markdown += '---\n\n';
   485→
   486→    conversationHistory.forEach(msg => {
   487→      const role = msg.role === 'user' ? 'You' : 'Assistant';
   488→      markdown += `## ${role}\n\n`;
   489→      markdown += msg.content + '\n\n';
   490→      markdown += '---\n\n';
   491→    });
   492→
   493→    // Create download link
   494→    const blob = new Blob([markdown], { type: 'text/markdown' });
   495→    const url = URL.createObjectURL(blob);
   496→    const a = document.createElement('a');
   497→    a.href = url;
   498→    a.download = `safecast-conversation-${new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)}.md`;
   499→    document.body.appendChild(a);
   500→    a.click();
   501→    document.body.removeChild(a);
   502→    URL.revokeObjectURL(url);
   503→  }
   504→
   505→  function clearConversation() {
   506→    // Clear conversation history
   507→    conversationHistory = [];
   508→
   509→    // Clear all messages
   510→    while (messagesEl.firstChild) {
   511→      messagesEl.removeChild(messagesEl.firstChild);
   512→    }
   513→
   514→    // Show intro again
   515→    messagesEl.appendChild(introEl);
   516→    introEl.style.display = 'block';
   517→
   518→    // Focus on input
   519→    msgEl.focus();
   520→  }
   521→
   522→  function sendMessage(text) {
   523→    busy = true;
   524→    sendBtn.disabled = true;
   525→
   526→    addMessage('user', text);
   527→    const botBubble = addMessage('bot', '…thinking…', 'thinking');
   528→
   529→    let accumulated = '';
   530→
   531→    function finish(success = true) {
   532→      botBubble.classList.remove('thinking');
   533→      busy = false;
   534→      sendBtn.disabled = false;
   535→      msgEl.focus();
   536→
   537→      // Add AI disclaimer to successful bot messages
   538→      if (success && accumulated) {
   539→        // Re-render with disclaimer
   540→        const disclaimerText = '\n\n---\n\n_Note: AI-generated response using data from the Safecast radiation monitoring network. For critical safety decisions, please consult official sources._';
   541→        botBubble.innerHTML = markdownToHTML(accumulated + disclaimerText);
   542→
   543→        // Re-add copy button (since we replaced innerHTML)
   544→        const copyBtn = document.createElement('button');
   545→        copyBtn.className = 'copy-btn';
   546→        copyBtn.textContent = 'Copy';
   547→        copyBtn.onclick = () => copyMessage(accumulated, copyBtn); // Copy without disclaimer
   548→        botBubble.appendChild(copyBtn);
   549→
   550→        conversationHistory.push(
   551→          { role: 'user', content: text },
   552→          { role: 'assistant', content: accumulated }
   553→        );
   554→
   555→        // Keep only last 10 messages (5 exchanges) to prevent rate limits
   556→        const MAX_HISTORY = 10;
   557→        if (conversationHistory.length > MAX_HISTORY) {
   558→          conversationHistory = conversationHistory.slice(-MAX_HISTORY);
   559→        }
   560→      }
   561→    }
   562→
   563→    fetch('/chat', {
   564→      method: 'POST',
   565→      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
   566→      body: JSON.stringify({
   567→        message: text,
   568→        history: conversationHistory
   569→      }),
   570→    }).then(response => {
   571→      const reader  = response.body.getReader();
   572→      const decoder = new TextDecoder();
   573→      let   buffer  = '';
   574→
   575→      function pump() {
   576→        return reader.read().then(({ done, value }) => {
   577→          if (done) { finish(); return; }
   578→
   579→          buffer += decoder.decode(value, { stream: true });
   580→          const lines = buffer.split('\n');
   581→          buffer = lines.pop(); // keep incomplete last line
   582→
   583→          for (const line of lines) {
   584→            if (!line.trim()) continue;
   585→            try {
   586→              const ev = JSON.parse(line);
   587→              if (ev.type === 'text') {
   588→                if (botBubble.classList.contains('thinking')) {
   589→                  botBubble.classList.remove('thinking');
   590→                  accumulated = '';
   591→                }
   592→                accumulated += ev.text;
   593→                botBubble.innerHTML = markdownToHTML(accumulated);
   594→                messagesEl.scrollTop = messagesEl.scrollHeight;
   595→              } else if (ev.type === 'done') {
   596→                finish();
   597→              } else if (ev.type === 'error') {
   598→                botBubble.classList.remove('thinking');
   599→                botBubble.classList.add('error');
   600→                botBubble.textContent = 'Error: ' + ev.error;
   601→                finish(false);
   602→              }
   603→            } catch { /* malformed line, skip */ }
   604→          }
   605→          return pump();
   606→        });
   607→      }
   608→      return pump();
   609→    }).catch(err => {
   610→      botBubble.classList.remove('thinking');
   611→      botBubble.classList.add('error');
   612→      botBubble.textContent = 'Connection error: ' + err.message;
   613→      finish(false);
   614→    });
   615→  }
   616→
   617→  msgEl.focus();
   618→</script>
   619→</body>
   620→</html>
   621→

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

> AGENT

Based on the web-chat directory, this is a complete web interface that connects Claude AI directly to the Safecast MCP server. Here's what it includes:

## [main.go](go/cmd/web-chat/main.go)
A standalone Go web server (port 3334) that:
- Connects to the MCP server at `localhost:3333/mcp-http`
- Uses Claude Sonnet 4.5 via the Anthropic API
- Implements an agentic loop where Claude can call MCP tools
- Streams responses as NDJSON for real-time display
- Handles CloudFront buffering for production deployment
- Includes comprehensive system prompt with tool selection guidance

## [index.html](go/cmd/web-chat/index.html)
A polished dark-themed chat interface with:
- **Markdown rendering** for tables, headers, links, code
- **Clickable sensor/track links** that open the map at specific coordinates
- **Copy button** on bot messages
- **Download conversation** feature (saves as .md file)
- **Conversation history** tracking (last 10 messages for context)
- **AI disclaimer** automatically added to all responses
- **Starter prompts** like "What's the radiation level near Tokyo?"

## Key System Prompt Features (lines 24-67)
- **Tool selection logic**: "current/latest" → sensor_current, "historical" → query_radiation
- **Search radius guidelines**: 500m for buildings, 15-25km for cities, 30-50km for Tokyo
- **Formatting rules**: Always use markdown tables for sensor/track data, include clickable map links
- **Data understanding**: Distinguishes real-time sensors vs historical mobile surveys

## Usage
```bash
cd go/cmd/web-chat
export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-...
export MCP_URL=http://localhost:3333/mcp-http  # optional
export CLAUDE_MODEL=claude-sonnet-4-5          # optional
export PORT=3334                               # optional
go run main.go
```

This provides a user-friendly way to interact with the Safecast data through Claude, without needing Claude Desktop or manual MCP configuration.

     1→# Safecast MCP Server connecting to simplemap.safecast.org V0.9
     2→
     3→An MCP (Model Context Protocol) server that exposes [Safecast](https://safecast.org) radiation monitoring data to AI assistants like Claude. The server provides 15 tools for querying both real-time sensor readings and historical radiation measurements, browsing sensor tracks, spectroscopy data, analytics, and educational reference data.
     4→
     5→## Features
     6→
     7→- **15 tools** for querying Safecast radiation data
     8→- **Real-time and historical data access**: Query both real-time sensor readings and historical measurements
     9→- **Dual transport**: SSE and Streamable HTTP (works with Claude.ai)
    10→- **PostgreSQL + PostGIS** for fast spatial queries (with REST API fallback)
    11→- **DuckDB analytics** for usage statistics and aggregate queries
    12→- **Structured runtime logging** for monitoring tool usage and performance
    13→- **Read-only** access to public Safecast data
    14→
    15→## Tools Overview
    16→
    17→| Tool | Data Type | Description |
    18→|------|-----------|-------------|
    19→| `query_radiation` | Historical | Find measurements near a lat/lon coordinate |
    20→| `search_area` | Historical | Search within a geographic bounding box |
    21→| `list_tracks` | Historical | Browse bGeigie Import tracks by year/month |
    22→| `get_track` | Historical | Get measurements from a specific track |
    23→| `device_history` | Mixed | Historical data from a monitoring device (supports both bGeigie and real-time sensors) |
    24→| `list_sensors` | Real-time | Discover active fixed sensors (Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen, etc.) by location or type |
    25→| `sensor_current` | Real-time | Get the latest reading(s) from a specific sensor or from all sensors in a geographic area |
    26→| `sensor_history` | Real-time | Pull time-series data from a fixed sensor over a date range |
    27→| `list_spectra` | Historical | Browse and search gamma spectroscopy records |
    28→| `get_spectrum` | Historical | Get full spectroscopy channel data for a measurement |
    29→| `radiation_info` | Reference | Educational reference (units, safety levels, detectors, isotopes) |
    30→| `radiation_stats` | Aggregate | Aggregate radiation statistics by year/month |
    31→| `query_analytics` | Analytics | Server usage statistics (call counts, durations) |
    32→| `db_info` | Diagnostic | Database connection and status (diagnostic) |
    33→| `ping` | Diagnostic | Health check |
    34→
    35→## Real-time Data Access
    36→
    37→The Safecast MCP server provides access to real-time radiation data from fixed sensors (Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen, etc.) through dedicated tools. These tools query the `realtime_measurements` table in the PostgreSQL database to retrieve the most current readings from active sensors.
    38→
    39→### Real-time Tools
    40→- `list_sensors`: Discover active fixed sensors by location or type
    41→- `sensor_current`: Get the latest reading(s) from specific sensors or geographic areas
    42→- `sensor_history`: Pull time-series data from fixed sensors over date ranges
    43→- `device_history`: Access both historical bGeigie data and real-time sensor data for a specific device
    44→
    45→> **Note**: Real-time data tools require a database connection to access the `realtime_measurements` table. These tools will fall back to the Safecast REST API if no database is configured.
    46→
    47→## Tool Reference
    48→
    49→### query_radiation
    50→
    51→Find radiation measurements near a geographic location. Returns measurements within a specified radius, sorted by most recent.
    52→
    53→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
    54→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
    55→| `lat` | number | Yes | | Latitude (-90 to 90) |
    56→| `lon` | number | Yes | | Longitude (-180 to 180) |
    57→| `radius_m` | number | No | 1500 | Search radius in meters (25 to 50,000) |
    58→| `limit` | number | No | 25 | Max results (1 to 10,000) |
    59→
    60→**Example**: Find measurements within 5km of Fukushima Daiichi:
    61→```json
    62→{"name": "query_radiation", "arguments": {"lat": 37.42, "lon": 141.03, "radius_m": 5000}}
    63→```
    64→
    65→Each result includes: `id`, `value` (dose rate in uSv/h), `captured_at`, `location` (lat/lon), `device_id`, `detector`, `track_id`, `has_spectrum`, and `distance_m`.
    66→
    67→---
    68→
    69→### search_area
    70→
    71→Find radiation measurements within a geographic bounding box.
    72→
    73→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
    74→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
    75→| `min_lat` | number | Yes | | Southern boundary latitude |
    76→| `max_lat` | number | Yes | | Northern boundary latitude |
    77→| `min_lon` | number | Yes | | Western boundary longitude |
    78→| `max_lon` | number | Yes | | Eastern boundary longitude |
    79→| `limit` | number | No | 100 | Max results (1 to 10,000) |
    80→
    81→**Example**: Search the Tokyo metropolitan area:
    82→```json
    83→{"name": "search_area", "arguments": {"min_lat": 35.5, "max_lat": 35.8, "min_lon": 139.5, "max_lon": 139.9}}
    84→```
    85→
    86→---
    87→
    88→### list_tracks
    89→
    90→Browse bGeigie Import tracks (bulk radiation measurement drives/journeys). Each track represents a set of measurements collected during a single bGeigie session.
    91→
    92→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
    93→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
    94→| `year` | number | No | | Filter by year (2000-2100) |
    95→| `month` | number | No | | Filter by month (1-12, requires `year`) |
    96→| `limit` | number | No | 50 | Max results (1 to 50,000) |
    97→
    98→**Example**: Browse tracks from January 2024:
    99→```json
   100→{"name": "list_tracks", "arguments": {"year": 2024, "month": 1}}
   101→```
   102→
   103→Each result includes: `track_id`, `name`, `description`, `city`, `measurement_count`, `created_at`, and status info.
   104→
   105→---
   106→
   107→### get_track
   108→
   109→Retrieve all radiation measurements from a specific track/journey. Use `list_tracks` to find track IDs first.
   110→
   111→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
   112→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
   113→| `track_id` | string | Yes | | Track identifier |
   114→| `from` | number | No | | Start marker ID for filtering |
   115→| `to` | number | No | | End marker ID for filtering |
   116→| `limit` | number | No | 200 | Max results (1 to 10,000) |
   117→
   118→**Example**: Get measurements from a specific track:
   119→```json
   120→{"name": "get_track", "arguments": {"track_id": "8eh5m1"}}
   121→```
   122→
   123→---
   124→
   125→### device_history
   126→
   127→Get historical radiation measurements from a specific monitoring device over a time period. This tool now supports both bGeigie import data and real-time sensor data.
   128→
   129→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
   130→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
   131→| `device_id` | string | Yes | | Device identifier |
   132→| `days` | number | No | 30 | Days of history (1 to 365) |
   133→| `limit` | number | No | 200 | Max results (1 to 10,000) |
   134→
   135→**Example**: Get 90 days of history from a device:
   136→```json
   137→{"name": "device_history", "arguments": {"device_id": "12345", "days": 90}}
   138→```
   139→
   140→> **Note**: This tool queries both the `markers` table (for bGeigie imports) and the `realtime_measurements` table (for fixed sensors) to provide a comprehensive history from the specified device.
   141→
   142→---
   143→
   144→### list_sensors
   145→
   146→Discover active fixed sensors (Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen, etc.) by location or type, returning device IDs, locations, status, and last reading timestamp.
   147→
   148→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
   149→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
   150→| `type` | string | No | | Filter by sensor type (e.g., 'Pointcast', 'Solarcast', 'bGeigieZen') |
   151→| `min_lat` | number | No | -90 | Southern boundary for geographic filter |
   152→| `max_lat` | number | No | 90 | Northern boundary for geographic filter |
   153→| `min_lon` | number | No | -180 | Western boundary for geographic filter |
   154→| `max_lon` | number | No | 180 | Eastern boundary for geographic filter |
   155→| `limit` | number | No | 50 | Max results (1 to 1000) |
   156→
   157→**Example**: Find all Pointcast sensors in Japan:
   158→```json
   159→{"name": "list_sensors", "arguments": {"type": "Pointcast", "min_lat": 30, "max_lat": 46, "min_lon": 129, "max_lon": 146}}
   160→```
   161→
   162→> **Note**: Requires database connection to access `realtime_measurements` table.
   163→
   164→---
   165→
   166→### sensor_current
   167→
   168→Get the latest reading(s) from a specific sensor or from all sensors in a geographic area.
   169→
   170→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
   171→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
   172→| `device_id` | string | No | | Specific device ID to get latest reading from |
   173→| `min_lat` | number | No | -90 | Southern boundary for geographic filter |
   174→| `max_lat` | number | No | 90 | Northern boundary for geographic filter |
   175→| `min_lon` | number | No | -180 | Western boundary for geographic filter |
   176→| `max_lon` | number | No | 180 | Eastern boundary for geographic filter |
   177→| `limit` | number | No | 25 | Max results (1 to 1000) |
   178→
   179→**Example**: Get latest reading from a specific sensor:
   180→```json
   181→{"name": "sensor_current", "arguments": {"device_id": "sensor-123"}}
   182→```
   183→
   184→**Example**: Get latest readings from all sensors in Tokyo:
   185→```json
   186→{"name": "sensor_current", "arguments": {"min_lat": 35.5, "max_lat": 35.8, "min_lon": 139.5, "max_lon": 139.9, "limit": 50}}
   187→```
   188→
   189→> **Note**: Requires database connection to access `realtime_measurements` table.
   190→
   191→---
   192→
   193→### sensor_history
   194→
   195→Pull time-series data from a fixed sensor over a date range.
   196→
   197→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
   198→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
   199→| `device_id` | string | Yes | | Device identifier to get historical data from |
   200→| `start_date` | string | Yes | | Start date in YYYY-MM-DD format |
   201→| `end_date` | string | No | Today | End date in YYYY-MM-DD format |
   202→| `limit` | number | No | 200 | Max results (1 to 10,000) |
   203→
   204→**Example**: Get 30 days of history from a sensor:
   205→```json
   206→{"name": "sensor_history", "arguments": {"device_id": "sensor-123", "start_date": "2024-01-01", "end_date": "2024-01-31"}}
   207→```
   208→
   209→> **Note**: Requires database connection to access `realtime_measurements` table.
   210→
   211→---
   212→
   213→### list_spectra
   214→
   215→Browse and search gamma spectroscopy records. Returns metadata (filename, device, energy range, location) **without** the full channel data. Use `get_spectrum` with a `marker_id` from the results to fetch full channel data.
   216→
   217→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
   218→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
   219→| `min_lat` | number | No | | Southern boundary (requires all 4 bbox params) |
   220→| `max_lat` | number | No | | Northern boundary (requires all 4 bbox params) |
   221→| `min_lon` | number | No | | Western boundary (requires all 4 bbox params) |
   222→| `max_lon` | number | No | | Eastern boundary (requires all 4 bbox params) |
   223→| `source_format` | string | No | | Filter by file format (e.g., `"spe"`, `"csv"`) |
   224→| `device_model` | string | No | | Filter by detector name (partial match) |
   225→| `track_id` | string | No | | Filter by track identifier (e.g., `"8eh5m1"`, `"8ZnI7f"`) |
   226→| `limit` | number | No | 50 | Max results (1 to 500) |
   227→
   228→**Example**: Find all SPE spectrum files:
   229→```json
   230→{"name": "list_spectra", "arguments": {"source_format": "spe"}}
   231→```
   232→
   233→**Example**: Browse all spectra (no filters):
   234→```json
   235→{"name": "list_spectra", "arguments": {}}
   236→```
   237→
   238→**Example**: Get all spectrum files for a specific track:
   239→```json
   240→{"name": "list_spectra", "arguments": {"track_id": "8eh5m1"}}
   241→```
   242→
   243→**Example**: Get all spectrum files regardless of track (no filters):
   244→```json
   245→{"name": "list_spectra", "arguments": {"limit": 100}}
   246→```
   247→
   248→Each result includes: `spectrum_id`, `marker_id`, `filename`, `source_format`, `device_model`, `channel_count`, `energy_range`, `live_time_sec`, `calibration`, `created_at`, and nested `marker` with location and track_id.
   249→
   250→> **Note**: Requires database connection. No REST API fallback.
   251→
   252→---
   253→
   254→### get_spectrum
   255→
   256→Get full gamma spectroscopy channel data for a specific measurement point. Returns the complete spectrum including all channel counts.
   257→
   258→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
   259→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
   260→| `marker_id` | number | Yes | | Marker/measurement identifier (get from `list_spectra`) |
   261→
   262→**Example**:
   263→```json
   264→{"name": "get_spectrum", "arguments": {"marker_id": 4902886}}
   265→```
   266→
   267→Returns: `channels` (array of counts), `channel_count`, `energy_min_kev`, `energy_max_kev`, `live_time_sec`, `real_time_sec`, `device_model`, `calibration`, `source_format`, `filename`, plus marker location and dose rate.
   268→
   269→---
   270→
   271→### radiation_info
   272→
   273→Get educational reference information about radiation. Returns static content.
   274→
   275→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
   276→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
   277→| `topic` | string | Yes | | One of: `units`, `dose_rates`, `safety_levels`, `detectors`, `background_levels`, `isotopes` |
   278→
   279→**Topics**:
   280→- `units` -- Explanation of uSv/h, CPM, Bq, Sv
   281→- `dose_rates` -- Typical dose rate ranges and what they mean
   282→- `safety_levels` -- International safety standards and thresholds
   283→- `detectors` -- Types of radiation detectors and how they work
   284→- `background_levels` -- Natural background radiation by region
   285→- `isotopes` -- Common radioactive isotopes and their properties
   286→
   287→---
   288→
   289→### radiation_stats
   290→
   291→Get aggregate radiation statistics from the Safecast database grouped by time interval. Powered by DuckDB + PostgreSQL.
   292→
   293→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
   294→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
   295→| `interval` | string | No | `"year"` | Aggregation: `"year"`, `"month"`, or `"overall"` |
   296→
   297→**Example**: Get yearly statistics:
   298→```json
   299→{"name": "radiation_stats", "arguments": {"interval": "year"}}
   300→```
   301→
   302→---
   303→
   304→### query_analytics
   305→
   306→Get usage statistics for all MCP tools including call counts, average duration, and max duration. Powered by DuckDB local logs. No parameters required.
   307→
   308→### Structured Runtime Logging
   309→
   310→The server includes comprehensive structured logging for monitoring AI tool usage and performance. The logging system is implemented via:
   311→
   312→- **`main.go`**: `instrument()` wrapper function that wraps each tool handler to capture execution metrics
   313→- **`duckdb_client.go`**: `LogQueryAsync()` function that asynchronously writes logs to DuckDB's `mcp_query_log` table
   314→
   315→The logging system:
   316→
   317→- Records timestamps, tool names, parameters, result counts, and query durations
   318→- Provides asynchronous logging that doesn't block tool execution
   319→- Stores logs in DuckDB for fast analytics via the `query_analytics` tool
   320→- Supports optional persistent storage via `DUCKDB_PATH` environment variable
   321→
   322→This enables better observability into how tools are being used and helps identify performance bottlenecks.
   323→
   324→---
   325→
   326→### db_info
   327→
   328→Diagnostic tool that returns database connection info, PostgreSQL version, replication status, and upload counts. No parameters required.
   329→
   330→---
   331→
   332→### ping
   333→
   334→Health check. Returns `"pong"`. No parameters required.
   335→
   336→## REST API
   337→
   338→The server also exposes a standard REST API on the same port as the MCP endpoints. All endpoints return JSON and are documented interactively via Swagger UI.
   339→
   340→| Method | Path | Description |
   341→|--------|------|-------------|
   342→| GET | `/api/radiation` | Find measurements near lat/lon |
   343→| GET | `/api/area` | Find measurements in a bounding box |
   344→| GET | `/api/tracks` | List bGeigie measurement tracks |
   345→| GET | `/api/track/{id}` | Get measurements from a track |
   346→| GET | `/api/device/{id}/history` | Device history (bGeigie + fixed sensors) |
   347→| GET | `/api/sensors` | List active fixed sensors |
   348→| GET | `/api/sensor/{id}/current` | Latest reading from a sensor |
   349→| GET | `/api/sensor/{id}/history` | Time-series from a sensor |
   350→| GET | `/api/spectra` | Browse gamma spectroscopy records |
   351→| GET | `/api/spectrum/{marker_id}` | Full spectroscopy channel data |
   352→| GET | `/api/stats` | Aggregate radiation statistics |
   353→| GET | `/api/info/{topic}` | Reference information (units, safety levels, etc.) |
   354→| GET | `/docs/` | Interactive Swagger UI |
   355→| GET | `/docs/doc.json` | Raw OpenAPI spec |
   356→
   357→**Quick example:**
   358→```bash
   359→curl "http://localhost:3333/api/radiation?lat=37.42&lon=141.03&radius_m=5000&limit=10"
   360→```
   361→
   362→### Updating API Documentation
   363→
   364→The Swagger docs are generated from `// @Summary`, `// @Param`, and `// @Router` annotations in the `rest_*.go` files. After changing any annotation, regenerate with:
   365→
   366→```bash
   367→# Install swag CLI (one-time)
   368→go install github.com/swaggo/swag/cmd/swag@latest
   369→
   370→# Regenerate from go/ directory
   371→cd go && swag init -g cmd/mcp-server/rest.go --dir cmd/mcp-server --output cmd/mcp-server/docs
   372→```
   373→
   374→The generated `docs/` folder is committed to the repo — the deployed binary does not need the swag CLI.
   375→
   376→## Quick Start
   377→
   378→```bash
   379→cd go
   380→go build -o safecast-mcp ./cmd/mcp-server/
   381→./safecast-mcp
   382→```
   383→
   384→The server listens on port 3333 by default. It serves both MCP protocol endpoints and the REST API.
   385→
   386→Open `http://localhost:3333/docs/` for the interactive Swagger UI.
   387→
   388→### Environment Variables
   389→
   390→| Variable | Required | Description |
   391→|----------|----------|-------------|
   392→| `MCP_BASE_URL` | No | Base URL advertised by the SSE transport so clients know where to POST messages back (default: `http://localhost:3333`). Must **not** include `/mcp` — the server appends that automatically. |
   393→| `DATABASE_URL` | No | PostgreSQL connection string. If not set, uses the Safecast REST API. |
   394→
   395→### Endpoints
   396→
   397→- **SSE**: `/mcp/sse` (GET) and `/mcp/message` (POST)
   398→- **Streamable HTTP**: `/mcp-http` (POST)
   399→
   400→## Connecting Claude to the MCP
   401→
   402→### Claude Code (CLI) - Recommended
   403→
   404→The fastest way to add the MCP server using Claude Code CLI:
   405→
   406→```bash
   407→# Add the production server
   408→claude mcp add --transport http safecast https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp-http
   409→
   410→# Or add with user scope (available in all projects)
   411→claude mcp add --transport http safecast --scope user https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp-http
   412→```
   413→
   414→**Management commands:**
   415→```bash
   416→claude mcp list              # List all configured servers
   417→claude mcp get safecast      # View server details
   418→claude mcp remove safecast   # Remove if needed
   419→```
   420→
   421→### Claude.ai (Web Interface)
   422→
   423→To connect via the web interface:
   424→
   425→1. Open [claude.ai](https://claude.ai) in your browser
   426→2. Go to **Settings** (bottom-left) > **Integrations**
   427→3. Click **Add more** > **Add custom integration**
   428→4. Enter a name (e.g. "Safecast") and paste the Streamable HTTP endpoint URL:
   429→   ```
   430→   https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp-http
   431→   ```
   432→5. Click **Save** — the Safecast tools will now be available in your conversations
   433→
   434→### Running Locally
   435→
   436→To connect Claude to a locally running MCP server:
   437→
   438→1. Start your local MCP server:
   439→   ```bash
   440→   cd go
   441→   go run ./cmd/mcp-server/
   442→   ```
   443→   
   444→2. The server will start on `http://localhost:3333` by default
   445→
   446→3. Expose your local server to the internet using a tool like ngrok:
   447→   ```bash
   448→   ngrok http 3333
   449→   ```
   450→   
   451→4. Take the HTTPS URL provided by ngrok (e.g., `https://abc123.ngrok.io`) and append `/mcp-http` to form the endpoint URL (e.g., `https://abc123.ngrok.io/mcp-http`)
   452→
   453→5. Follow steps 2-5 from the "Using the Deployed Server" section above, using your ngrok URL instead
   454→
   455→### Configuration Notes
   456→
   457→- The MCP server supports both SSE and Streamable HTTP transports
   458→- The Streamable HTTP endpoint (`/mcp-http`) is recommended for Claude integration
   459→- If using a custom domain or different port, adjust the endpoint URL accordingly
   460→- Make sure your server is accessible from the internet for Claude to connect
   461→
   462→## Architecture
   463→
   464→```
   465→Claude / AI client
   466→    |
   467→    v
   468→MCP Server (Go, mcp-go)
   469→    |
   470→    +---> Tool Execution with Structured Logging
   471→          |
   472→          +---> PostgreSQL + PostGIS (primary, if DATABASE_URL set)
   473→          |     |
   474→          |     +---> markers table (historical bGeigie data)
   475→          |     |
   476→          |     +---> realtime_measurements table (real-time sensor data)
   477→          |     |
   478→          |     +---> spectra table (spectroscopy data)
   479→          |
   480→          +---> DuckDB Analytics Engine
   481→          |     |
   482→          |     +---> Local usage statistics
   483→          |
   484→          +---> Structured Logging System
   485→                |
   486→                +---> Session tracking
   487→                |
   488→                +---> Performance metrics
   489→                |
   490→                +---> Error logging
   491→                |
   492→                +---> Optional external export
   493→    |
   494→    +---> simplemap.safecast.org REST API (fallback)
   495→```
   496→
   497→The server uses [`mcp-go`](https://github.com/mark3labs/mcp-go) for MCP protocol support. All tools attempt a direct database query first and fall back to the Safecast REST API if no database is configured or the query fails. Real-time data tools specifically query the `realtime_measurements` table for current sensor readings.
   498→
   499→## Project Structure
   500→
   501→```
   502→go/cmd/mcp-server/
   503→  main.go              # Server setup, tool registration, dual transport, instrumentation
   504→  api_client.go        # Safecast REST API client
   505→  db_client.go         # PostgreSQL connection pool (pgx)
   506→  duckdb_client.go     # DuckDB analytics engine, async logging (LogQueryAsync)
   507→  reference_data.go    # Static radiation reference data
   508→
   509→  # MCP Tools
   510→  tool_query_radiation.go
   511→  tool_search_area.go
   512→  tool_list_tracks.go
   513→  tool_get_track.go
   514→  tool_device_history.go
   515→  tool_get_spectrum.go
   516→  tool_list_spectra.go
   517→  tool_radiation_info.go
   518→  tool_list_sensors.go
   519→  tool_sensor_current.go
   520→  tool_sensor_history.go
   521→  tool_analytics.go    # query_analytics, radiation_stats tools
   522→  tool_db_info.go
   523→
   524→  # REST API
   525→  rest.go              # REST handler, Swagger UI, theme CSS
   526→  rest_radiation.go
   527→  rest_area.go
   528→  rest_tracks.go
   529→  rest_device.go
   530→  rest_sensors.go
   531→  rest_spectra.go
   532→  rest_stats.go
   533→  rest_info.go
   534→
   535→  # Generated Documentation
   536→  docs/
   537→    docs.go            # Generated by swag init
   538→    swagger.json
   539→    swagger.yaml
   540→
   541→  # Static Assets
   542→  static/
   543→    favicon.ico
   544→    favicon-16x16.png
   545→    favicon-32x32.png
   546→```
   547→
   548→## Development
   549→
   550→```bash
   551→cd go
   552→go run ./cmd/mcp-server/
   553→```
   554→
   555→Cross-compile for Linux deployment:
   556→
   557→```bash
   558→cd go
   559→GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 go build -o safecast-mcp ./cmd/mcp-server/
   560→```
   561→
   562→## Deployment
   563→
   564→Pushing to `main` automatically builds and deploys to **simplemap.safecast.org** (the map server) via GitHub Actions (only when Go source files, `go.mod`/`go.sum`, or the workflow itself change).
   565→
   566→> **Important**: The domain uses CloudFront CDN. SSH deployment must use the server IP (65.108.24.131), not the domain name. See [CloudFront Deployment Guide](docs/CLOUDFRONT_DEPLOYMENT.md) for details.
   567→
   568→> **Note**: The MCP server runs on the same server as the database for optimal performance (localhost connections eliminate network latency). See [MIGRATION_TO_MAP_SERVER.md](MIGRATION_TO_MAP_SERVER.md) for migration details.
   569→
   570→### How it works
   571→
   572→1. GitHub Action cross-compiles the Go binary
   573→2. Uploads it to the map server via rsync
   574→3. Creates `.env` file with localhost database connection
   575→4. Restarts the MCP server via systemd
   576→5. Configures Apache proxy for `/mcp-http`, `/docs/`, and `/api/` endpoints
   577→6. Runs a health check against the `/mcp-http` endpoint
   578→
   579→### Setting up secrets
   580→
   581→The GitHub Action requires two repository secrets. Go to **Settings** > **Secrets and variables** > **Actions** and add:
   582→
   583→| Secret | Description |
   584→|--------|-------------|
   585→| `SSH_PRIVATE_KEY` | SSH private key with access to the map server (ed25519 format) |
   586→| `MAP_SERVER_HOST` | Map server IP address: **`65.108.24.131`** (use IP, not domain) |
   587→
   588→**⚠️ CRITICAL:** Use the IP address `65.108.24.131` for `MAP_SERVER_HOST`, **not** `simplemap.safecast.org`. The domain uses CloudFront CDN which doesn't handle SSH traffic.
   589→
   590→To generate a deploy key:
   591→
   592→```bash
   593→ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -C "github-deploy-mcp@simplemap" -f ~/.ssh/safecast-mcp-deploy -N ""
   594→```
   595→
   596→Then add the public key to the map server (use IP address):
   597→
   598→```bash
   599→ssh-copy-id -i ~/.ssh/safecast-mcp-deploy.pub root@65.108.24.131
   600→```
   601→
   602→And paste the contents of `~/.ssh/safecast-mcp-deploy` (the private key) as the `SSH_PRIVATE_KEY` secret in GitHub.
   603→
   604→### Manual deploy
   605→
   606→**⚠️ IMPORTANT:** The domain `simplemap.safecast.org` uses CloudFront CDN, which only handles HTTP/HTTPS traffic. For SSH/SCP deployment, you **must use the server IP address** (65.108.24.131), not the domain name.
   607→
   608→```bash
   609→cd go
   610→GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 go build -o safecast-mcp ./cmd/mcp-server/
   611→scp safecast-mcp root@65.108.24.131:/root/safecast-mcp-server/
   612→ssh root@65.108.24.131 "systemctl restart safecast-mcp"
   613→```
   614→
   615→**Why IP address?** CloudFront operates on ports 80/443 (HTTP/HTTPS) and doesn't forward SSH traffic (port 22). When you SSH to the domain name, it tries to connect to CloudFront's servers, not the actual server.
   616→
   617→## Contributing
   618→
   619→Contributions welcome. If changing a tool's interface, please open an issue first. Fork, branch, PR.
   620→
   621→## License
   622→
   623→MIT
   624→
   625→## Technical Implementation Details
   626→
   627→### CORS Processing
   628→The application handles cross-origin resource sharing through standard HTTP headers managed by the underlying `mcp-go` library.
   629→
   630→### Logarithmic Scaling
   631→Used for mapping radiation values to visual properties like marker opacity and color intensity for better visualization of wide-ranging values.
   632→
   633→### Timestamp Conversion
   634→The application converts Unix timestamps from the API to human-readable formats for display in map popups.
   635→
   636→### Responsive Design
   637→The application adapts to various screen sizes using responsive CSS techniques.
   638→
   639→## API Integration Details
   640→
   641→- **Endpoints Used**: 
   642→  - `/mcp/sse` (SSE transport)
   643→  - `/mcp/message` (message transport) 
   644→  - `/mcp-http` (streamable HTTP transport)
   645→- **Data Types**: JSON responses containing radiation measurements, sensor information, and spectroscopy data
   646→- **Request Methods**: Both GET (for SSE) and POST (for message passing) are supported
   647→
   648→## Browser Compatibility Requirements
   649→
   650→- HTML5/CSS3/ES5+ compatible browsers
   651→- WebSocket support for SSE communication
   652→- Modern JavaScript engine for processing MCP protocol messages
   653→
   654→## Deployment Notes
   655→
   656→- The application **must** be served via HTTP(S) server - direct file opening in browsers will not work due to CORS restrictions and API communication requirements.
   657→- Requires a backend server to handle MCP protocol communication with AI clients like Claude.
   658→
   659→<a href="https://www.paypal.com/ncp/payment/MAXS4ZUSGPDD4">
   660→  <img src="https://safecast.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/Donation-PayPal-1.png" border="0" alt="Donate with PayPal" />
   661→</a>

> AGENT

Yes, you're right! The deployment workflow configures the web-chat to use **Haiku 4.5** on the production server.

Looking at [.github/workflows/deploy.yml:61](.github/workflows/deploy.yml#L61), the deployment sets:
```bash
CLAUDE_MODEL=claude-haiku-4-5-20251001
```

However, [go/cmd/web-chat/main.go:379-381](go/cmd/web-chat/main.go#L379-L381) has a default fallback to Sonnet 4.5 if the environment variable isn't set:
```go
model := os.Getenv("CLAUDE_MODEL")
if model == "" {
    model = "claude-sonnet-4-5"
}
```

So the production web-chat on **simplemap.safecast.org** runs with **Haiku 4.5**, which makes sense because:
- **Faster responses** - Lower latency for users
- **Lower cost** - Haiku is significantly cheaper than Sonnet
- **Sufficient capability** - For querying radiation data and formatting tables, Haiku handles it well

The MCP server itself doesn't use any Claude model - it's just the server that provides tools. The model choice only matters for the web-chat client that calls Claude via the Anthropic API.

   124→// pingHandler is the health check tool implementation.
   125→func pingHandler(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
   126→	return mcp.NewToolResultText("pong"), nil
   127→}
   128→
   129→func instrument(
   130→	name string,
   131→	h func(context.Context, mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error),
   132→) func(context.Context, mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
   133→
   134→	return func(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
   135→
   136→		start := time.Now()
   137→
   138→		// Extract user info from MCP request arguments
   139→		userID := ""
   140→		userEmail := ""
   141→
   142→		if req.Params.Arguments != nil {
   143→
   144→			if args, ok := req.Params.Arguments.(map[string]any); ok {
   145→
   146→				if v, ok := args["user_id"].(string); ok {
   147→					userID = v
   148→				}
   149→
   150→				if v, ok := args["user_email"].(string); ok {
   151→					userEmail = v
   152→				}
   153→			}
   154→		}
   155→
   156→		// Execute tool
   157→		res, err := h(ctx, req)
   158→
   159→		duration := time.Since(start)
   160→
   161→		// Existing DuckDB analytics log
   162→		resultCount := 0
   163→		if res != nil {
   164→			resultCount = len(res.Content)
   165→		}
   166→
   167→		args := map[string]any{}
   168→		if req.Params.Arguments != nil {
   169→			if argsMap, ok := req.Params.Arguments.(map[string]any); ok {
   170→				args = argsMap
   171→			}
   172→		}
   173→
   174→		LogQueryAsync(name, args, resultCount, duration, "claude-client")
   175→
   176→		logAISessionWithUser(
   177→			name,
   178→			"",
   179→			duration.Milliseconds(),
   180→			err,
   181→			userID,
   182→			userEmail,
   183→		)
   184→
   185→		return res, err
   186→	}
   187→}

## ✅ **Improvements Made**

### 1. **New Tool: `query_extreme_readings`**
   - Directly finds the highest or lowest radiation readings with full location details
   - Solves the exact frustration shown in your screenshot
   - Supports optional geographic filtering
   - Returns up to 100 results with device ID, location, timestamp, and track ID

### 2. **Updated Web-Chat System Prompt**
   - Now guides the assistant to use `query_extreme_readings` when users ask about "highest", "maximum", "extreme" readings
   - Explicitly warns NOT to use `radiation_stats` for location-specific queries

### 3. **Registered in Main Server**
   - Tool is now available as soon as you deploy

## 📋 **Additional Improvements to Consider**

### **A. Update the README to document the new tool:**
```markdown
### query_extreme_readings

Find the highest or lowest radiation readings in the database with full location details.

| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `direction` | string | No | "highest" | "highest" or "lowest" |
| `limit` | number | No | 10 | Max results (1 to 100) |
| `min_lat` | number | No | -90 | Optional geographic filter |
| `max_lat` | number | No | 90 | Optional geographic filter |
| `min_lon` | number | No | -180 | Optional geographic filter |
| `max_lon` | number | No | 180 | Optional geographic filter |

**Example**: Find the 20 highest readings globally:
```json
{"name": "query_extreme_readings", "arguments": {"direction": "highest", "limit": 20}}
```
```

```
