   287→---
   288→
   289→### radiation_stats
   290→
   291→Get aggregate radiation statistics from the Safecast database grouped by time interval. Powered by DuckDB + PostgreSQL.
   292→
   293→| Parameter | Type | Required | Default | Description |
   294→|-----------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
   295→| `interval` | string | No | `"year"` | Aggregation: `"year"`, `"month"`, or `"overall"` |
   296→
   297→**Example**: Get yearly statistics:
   298→```json
   299→{"name": "radiation_stats", "arguments": {"interval": "year"}}
   300→```
   301→
   302→---
   303→
   304→### query_analytics
   305→
   306→Get usage statistics for all MCP tools including call counts, average duration, and max duration. Powered by DuckDB local logs. No parameters required.
   307→
   308→### Structured Runtime Logging
   309→
   310→The server includes comprehensive structured logging for monitoring AI tool usage and performance. The logging system is implemented via:
   311→
   312→- **`main.go`**: `instrument()` wrapper function that wraps each tool handler to capture execution metrics
   313→- **`duckdb_client.go`**: `LogQueryAsync()` function that asynchronously writes logs to DuckDB's `mcp_query_log` table
   314→
   315→The logging system:
   316→
   317→- Records timestamps, tool names, parameters, result counts, and query durations
   318→- Provides asynchronous logging that doesn't block tool execution
   319→- Stores logs in DuckDB for fast analytics via the `query_analytics` tool
   320→- Supports optional persistent storage via `DUCKDB_PATH` environment variable
   321→
   322→This enables better observability into how tools are being used and helps identify performance bottlenecks.
   323→
   324→---
   325→
   326→### db_info
   327→
   328→Diagnostic tool that returns database connection info, PostgreSQL version, replication status, and upload counts. No parameters required.
   329→
   330→---
   331→
   332→### ping
   333→
   334→Health check. Returns `"pong"`. No parameters required.
   335→
   336→## REST API

     1→# Safecast MCP Server connecting to simplemap.safecast.org V0.9
     2→
     3→An MCP (Model Context Protocol) server that exposes [Safecast](https://safecast.org) radiation monitoring data to AI assistants like Claude. The server provides 15 tools for querying both real-time sensor readings and historical radiation measurements, browsing sensor tracks, spectroscopy data, analytics, and educational reference data.
     4→
     5→## Features
     6→
     7→- **15 tools** for querying Safecast radiation data
     8→- **Real-time and historical data access**: Query both real-time sensor readings and historical measurements
     9→- **Dual transport**: SSE and Streamable HTTP (works with Claude.ai)
    10→- **PostgreSQL + PostGIS** for fast spatial queries (with REST API fallback)
    11→- **DuckDB analytics** for usage statistics and aggregate queries
    12→- **Structured runtime logging** for monitoring tool usage and performance
    13→- **Read-only** access to public Safecast data
    14→
    15→## Tools Overview
    16→
    17→| Tool | Data Type | Description |
    18→|------|-----------|-------------|
    19→| `query_radiation` | Historical | Find measurements near a lat/lon coordinate |
    20→| `search_area` | Historical | Search within a geographic bounding box |
    21→| `list_tracks` | Historical | Browse bGeigie Import tracks by year/month |
    22→| `get_track` | Historical | Get measurements from a specific track |
    23→| `device_history` | Mixed | Historical data from a monitoring device (supports both bGeigie and real-time sensors) |
    24→| `list_sensors` | Real-time | Discover active fixed sensors (Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen, etc.) by location or type |
    25→| `sensor_current` | Real-time | Get the latest reading(s) from a specific sensor or from all sensors in a geographic area |
    26→| `sensor_history` | Real-time | Pull time-series data from a fixed sensor over a date range |
    27→| `list_spectra` | Historical | Browse and search gamma spectroscopy records |
    28→| `get_spectrum` | Historical | Get full spectroscopy channel data for a measurement |
    29→| `radiation_info` | Reference | Educational reference (units, safety levels, detectors, isotopes) |
    30→| `radiation_stats` | Aggregate | Aggregate radiation statistics by year/month |
    31→| `query_analytics` | Analytics | Server usage statistics (call counts, durations) |
    32→| `db_info` | Diagnostic | Database connection and status (diagnostic) |
    33→| `ping` | Diagnostic | Health check |
    34→
    35→## Real-time Data Access

{
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-map-MCP/go/cmd/mcp-server/rest_extreme.go",
  "content": "package main\n\nimport (\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"strconv\"\n\n\t\"github.com/mark3labs/mcp-go/mcp\"\n)\n\n// @Summary Find extreme radiation readings\n// @Description Find the highest or lowest radiation readings in the database with full location details\n// @Tags analytics\n// @Produce json\n// @Param direction query string false \"Direction: 'highest' or 'lowest'\" default(highest)\n// @Param limit query int false \"Number of results (1-100)\" default(10)\n// @Param min_lat query number false \"Southern boundary for geographic filter\" default(-90)\n// @Param max_lat query number false \"Northern boundary for geographic filter\" default(90)\n// @Param min_lon query number false \"Western boundary for geographic filter\" default(-180)\n// @Param max_lon query number false \"Eastern boundary for geographic filter\" default(180)\n// @Success 200 {object} map[string]interface{} \"Extreme readings with location details\"\n// @Failure 400 {object} map[string]string \"Invalid parameters\"\n// @Failure 500 {object} map[string]string \"Server error\"\n// @Router /api/extreme [get]\nfunc handleRESTExtremeReadings(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\tdirection := r.URL.Query().Get(\"direction\")\n\tif direction == \"\" {\n\t\tdirection = \"highest\"\n\t}\n\n\tlimit := 10\n\tif limitStr := r.URL.Query().Get(\"limit\"); limitStr != \"\" {\n\t\tif parsed, err := strconv.Atoi(limitStr); err == nil {\n\t\t\tlimit = parsed\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tminLat := -90.0\n\tif v := r.URL.Query().Get(\"min_lat\"); v != \"\" {\n\t\tif parsed, err := strconv.ParseFloat(v, 64); err == nil {\n\t\t\tminLat = parsed\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tmaxLat := 90.0\n\tif v := r.URL.Query().Get(\"max_lat\"); v != \"\" {\n\t\tif parsed, err := strconv.ParseFloat(v, 64); err == nil {\n\t\t\tmaxLat = parsed\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tminLon := -180.0\n\tif v := r.URL.Query().Get(\"min_lon\"); v != \"\" {\n\t\tif parsed, err := strconv.ParseFloat(v, 64); err == nil {\n\t\t\tminLon = parsed\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tmaxLon := 180.0\n\tif v := r.URL.Query().Get(\"max_lon\"); v != \"\" {\n\t\tif parsed, err := strconv.ParseFloat(v, 64); err == nil {\n\t\t\tmaxLon = parsed\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Create MCP request\n\treq := mcp.CallToolRequest{}\n\treq.Params.Name = \"query_extreme_readings\"\n\treq.Params.Arguments = map[string]any{\n\t\t\"direction\": direction,\n\t\t\"limit\":     float64(limit),\n\t\t\"min_lat\":   minLat,\n\t\t\"max_lat\":   maxLat,\n\t\t\"min_lon\":   minLon,\n\t\t\"max_lon\":   maxLon,\n\t}\n\n\tresult, err := handleQueryExtremeReadings(r.Context(), req)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, err.Error(), http.StatusInternalServerError)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif result.IsError != nil && *result.IsError {\n\t\tw.WriteHeader(http.StatusBadRequest)\n\t}\n\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\tjson.NewEncoder(w).Encode(extractJSONResult(result))\n}\n"
}

   336→### Structured Runtime Logging
   337→
   338→The server includes comprehensive structured logging for monitoring AI tool usage and performance. The logging system is implemented via:
   339→
   340→- **`main.go`**: `instrument()` wrapper function that wraps each tool handler to capture execution metrics
   341→- **`duckdb_client.go`**: `LogQueryAsync()` function that asynchronously writes logs to DuckDB's `mcp_query_log` table
   342→
   343→The logging system:
   344→
   345→- Records timestamps, tool names, parameters, result counts, and query durations
   346→- Provides asynchronous logging that doesn't block tool execution
   347→- Stores logs in DuckDB for fast analytics via the `query_analytics` tool
   348→- Supports optional persistent storage via `DUCKDB_PATH` environment variable
   349→
   350→This enables better observability into how tools are being used and helps identify performance bottlenecks.
   351→
   352→---
   353→
   354→### db_info
   355→
   356→Diagnostic tool that returns database connection info, PostgreSQL version, replication status, and upload counts. No parameters required.
   357→
   358→---
   359→
   360→### ping
   361→
   362→Health check. Returns `"pong"`. No parameters required.
   363→

   365→

   364→## REST API
   365→
   366→The server also exposes a standard REST API on the same port as the MCP endpoints. All endpoints return JSON and are documented interactively via Swagger UI.
   367→
   368→| Method | Path | Description |
   369→|--------|------|-------------|
   370→| GET | `/api/radiation` | Find measurements near lat/lon |
   371→| GET | `/api/area` | Find measurements in a bounding box |
   372→| GET | `/api/tracks` | List bGeigie measurement tracks |
   373→| GET | `/api/track/{id}` | Get measurements from a track |
   374→| GET | `/api/device/{id}/history` | Device history (bGeigie + fixed sensors) |
   375→| GET | `/api/sensors` | List active fixed sensors |
   376→| GET | `/api/sensor/{id}/current` | Latest reading from a sensor |
   377→| GET | `/api/sensor/{id}/history` | Time-series from a sensor |
   378→| GET | `/api/spectra` | Browse gamma spectroscopy records |
   379→| GET | `/api/spectrum/{marker_id}` | Full spectroscopy channel data |
   380→| GET | `/api/stats` | Aggregate radiation statistics |
   381→| GET | `/api/info/{topic}` | Reference information (units, safety levels, etc.) |
   382→| GET | `/docs/` | Interactive Swagger UI |
   383→| GET | `/docs/doc.json` | Raw OpenAPI spec |
   384→
   385→**Quick example:**
   386→```bash
   387→curl "http://localhost:3333/api/radiation?lat=37.42&lon=141.03&radius_m=5000&limit=10"
   388→```

     1→package main

     9→// handleStats handles GET /api/stats
    10→//
    11→// @Summary     Get aggregate radiation statistics
    12→// @Description Returns aggregate radiation dose-rate statistics from the Safecast database grouped by time interval. Powered by DuckDB + PostgreSQL.
    13→// @Tags        reference
    14→// @Produce     json
    15→// @Param       interval query string false "Aggregation interval: year, month, or overall" Enums(year, month, overall) default(year)
    16→// @Success     200 {object} map[string]interface{} "Statistics data with interval and source metadata"
    17→// @Failure     400 {object} map[string]string "Invalid interval value"
    18→// @Failure     503 {object} map[string]string "Analytics engine unavailable"
    19→// @Router      /stats [get]
    20→func (h *RESTHandler) handleStats(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    21→	if r.Method != http.MethodGet {
    22→		writeError(w, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, "method not allowed")
    23→		return
    24→	}
    25→
    26→	interval := r.URL.Query().Get("interval")
    27→	if interval == "" {
    28→		interval = "year"
    29→	}
    30→	switch interval {
    31→	case "year", "month", "overall":
    32→		// valid
    33→	default:
    34→		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "interval must be one of: year, month, overall")
    35→		return
    36→	}
    37→
    38→	// Construct a minimal MCP request and reuse the existing handler.
    39→	req := mcp.CallToolRequest{}
    40→	req.Params.Arguments = map[string]any{"interval": interval}
    41→	result, err := handleRadiationStats(r.Context(), req)
    42→	serveMCPResult(w, result, err)
    43→}
    44→

   385→
   386→**Quick example:**
   387→```bash

   389→```
   390→
   391→### Updating API Documentation
   392→
   393→The Swagger docs are generated from `// @Summary`, `// @Param`, and `// @Router` annotations in the `rest_*.go` files. After changing any annotation, regenerate with:
   394→
   395→```bash
   396→# Install swag CLI (one-time)
   397→go install github.com/swaggo/swag/cmd/swag@latest
   398→
   399→# Regenerate from go/ directory
   400→cd go && swag init -g cmd/mcp-server/rest.go --dir cmd/mcp-server --output cmd/mcp-server/docs
   401→```
   402→
   403→The generated `docs/` folder is committed to the repo — the deployed binary does not need the swag CLI.
   404→
   405→## Quick Start
   406→
   407→```bash
   408→cd go
   409→go build -o safecast-mcp ./cmd/mcp-server/
   410→./safecast-mcp
   411→```
   412→
   413→The server listens on port 3333 by default. It serves both MCP protocol endpoints and the REST API.
   414→
   415→Open `http://localhost:3333/docs/` for the interactive Swagger UI.
   416→
   417→### Environment Variables
   418→
   419→| Variable | Required | Description |
   420→|----------|----------|-------------|
   421→| `MCP_BASE_URL` | No | Base URL advertised by the SSE transport so clients know where to POST messages back (default: `http://localhost:3333`). Must **not** include `/mcp` — the server appends that automatically. |
   422→| `DATABASE_URL` | No | PostgreSQL connection string. If not set, uses the Safecast REST API. |
   423→
   424→### Endpoints
   425→
   426→- **SSE**: `/mcp/sse` (GET) and `/mcp/message` (POST)
   427→- **Streamable HTTP**: `/mcp-http` (POST)
   428→
   429→## Connecting Claude to the MCP
   430→
   431→### Claude Code (CLI) - Recommended
   432→
   433→The fastest way to add the MCP server using Claude Code CLI:
   434→
   435→```bash
   436→# Add the production server
   437→claude mcp add --transport http safecast https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp-http
   438→
   439→# Or add with user scope (available in all projects)
   440→claude mcp add --transport http safecast --scope user https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp-http
   441→```
   442→
   443→**Management commands:**
   444→```bash
   445→claude mcp list              # List all configured servers
   446→claude mcp get safecast      # View server details
   447→claude mcp remove safecast   # Remove if needed
   448→```
   449→
   450→### Claude.ai (Web Interface)
   451→
   452→To connect via the web interface:
   453→
   454→1. Open [claude.ai](https://claude.ai) in your browser
   455→2. Go to **Settings** (bottom-left) > **Integrations**
   456→3. Click **Add more** > **Add custom integration**
   457→4. Enter a name (e.g. "Safecast") and paste the Streamable HTTP endpoint URL:
   458→   ```
   459→   https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp-http
   460→   ```
   461→5. Click **Save** — the Safecast tools will now be available in your conversations
   462→
   463→### Running Locally
   464→

The assistant uses **Claude Haiku 4.5** for fast, cost-effective responses while accessing the full MCP toolset.\n\n**Try it now:** [https://simplemap.safecast.org/](https://simplemap.safecast.org/) *(web chat interface)*\n\n### Sample Questions\n\nThe assistant can help with a wide variety of radiation-related queries:\n\n**Real-time Sensor Data:**\n- \"What's the current radiation level near Tokyo?\"\n- \"Show me all active sensors in Fukushima prefecture\"\n- \"What are the latest readings in Osaka?\"\n- \"Is there a sensor near my coordinates 35.6762, 139.6503?\"\n\n**Historical Measurements:**\n- \"What are the highest radiation readings ever recorded?\"\n- \"Show me measurements from Fukushima in March 2011\"\n- \"Find radiation data near Chernobyl\"\n- \"What was the average radiation level in Tokyo in 2020?\"\n\n**Sensor Trends & Time Series:**\n- \"Show me the radiation trend for sensor pointcast:10042 over the past week\"\n- \"How has radiation changed at this location over the past year?\"\n- \"Compare radiation levels between 2012 and 2024\"\n\n**Spectroscopy Data:**\n- \"Find gamma spectroscopy measurements near Fukushima\"\n- \"Show me spectrum files from track 8eh5m1\"\n- \"What isotopes were detected in this area?\"\n\n**Aggregate Statistics:**\n- \"How does radiation compare year over year?\"\n- \"What's the global average background radiation?\"\n- \"Show me monthly statistics for 2023\"\n\n**Educational & Reference:**\n- \"What's the difference between µSv/h and CPM?\"\n- \"What are safe radiation levels?\"\n- \"Explain how    - Supports optional geographic filtering

### **2. REST API Endpoint**
   - **URL**: `GET /api/extreme`
   - **File**: [go/cmd/mcp-server/rest_extreme.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/rest_extreme.go)

    13→	mcp.WithDescription("Find the highest or lowest radiation readings in the database with full location details. Use this to identify extreme measurements globally or within a specific region."),
    14→	mcp.WithString("direction",
    15→		mcp.Description("'highest' for maximum readings or 'lowest' for minimum readings"),

    35→
    36→// Handler
    37→
    38→func handleQueryExtremeReadings(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    39→	if duckDB == nil {
    40→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("DuckDB analytics engine is not initialized"), nil
    41→	}
    42→
    43→	direction := req.GetString("direction", "highest")
    44→	limit := req.GetInt("limit", 10)
    45→	if limit < 1 || limit > 100 {
    46→		limit = 10
    47→	}
    48→
    49→	// Build query
    50→	orderDir := "DESC"
    51→	if direction == "lowest" {
    52→		orderDir = "ASC"
    53→	}
    54→
    55→	// Check for geographic filter
    56→	hasGeoFilter := false
    57→	minLat := req.GetFloat("min_lat", -90)
    58→	maxLat := req.GetFloat("max_lat", 90)
    59→	minLon := req.GetFloat("min_lon", -180)
    60→	maxLon := req.GetFloat("max_lon", 180)
    61→
    62→	if minLat != -90 || maxLat != 90 || minLon != -180 || maxLon != 180 {
    63→		hasGeoFilter = true
    64→	}
    65→
    66→	var query string
    67→	if hasGeoFilter {
    68→		query = fmt.Sprintf(`
    69→			SELECT
    70→				id,
    71→				doserate,
    72→				latitude,
    73→				longitude,
    74→				device_id,
    75→				to_timestamp(date)::TIMESTAMP AS captured_at,
    76→				track_id,
    77→				detector
    78→			FROM postgres_db.public.markers
    79→			WHERE doserate > 0 AND doserate < 10000
    80→			  AND latitude BETWEEN %.6f AND %.6f
    81→			  AND longitude BETWEEN %.6f AND %.6f
    82→			ORDER BY doserate %s
    83→			LIMIT %d
    84→		`, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, orderDir, limit)
    85→	} else {
    86→		query = fmt.Sprintf(`
    87→			SELECT
    88→				id,
    89→				doserate,
    90→				latitude,
    91→				longitude,
    92→				device_id,
    93→				to_timestamp(date)::TIMESTAMP AS captured_at,
    94→				track_id,
    95→				detector
    96→			FROM postgres_db.public.markers
    97→			WHERE doserate > 0 AND doserate < 10000
    98→			ORDER BY doserate %s
    99→			LIMIT %d
   100→		`, orderDir, limit)
   101→	}
   102→
   103→	// Execute query
   104→	rows, err := duckDB.Query(query)
   105→	if err != nil {
   106→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(fmt.Sprintf("Query failed: %v", err)), nil
   107→	}
   108→	defer rows.Close()
   109→
   110→	var results []map[string]any
   111→	for rows.Next() {
   112→		var id int64
   113→		var doserate float64
   114→		var lat, lon float64
   115→		var deviceID, trackID, detector *string
   116→		var capturedAt any
   117→
   118→		if err := rows.Scan(&id, &doserate, &lat, &lon, &deviceID, &capturedAt, &trackID, &detector); err != nil {
   119→			continue
   120→		}
   121→
   122→		result := map[string]any{
   123→			"id":       id,
   124→			"value":    doserate,
   125→			"unit":     "µSv/h",
   126→			"location": map[string]float64{"lat": lat, "lon": lon},
   127→		}
   128→
   129→		if capturedAt != nil {
   130→			result["captured_at"] = fmt.Sprintf("%v", capturedAt)
   131→		}
   132→		if deviceID != nil {
   133→			result["device_id"] = *deviceID
   134→		}
   135→		if trackID != nil {
   136→			result["track_id"] = *trackID
   137→		}
   138→		if detector != nil {
   139→			result["detector"] = *detector
   140→		}
   141→
   142→		results = append(results, result)
   143→	}
   144→
   145→	return jsonResult(map[string]any{
   146→		"direction":          direction,
   147→		"readings":           results,
   148→		"count":              len(results),
   149→		"source":             "duckdb_postgres_attach",
   150→		"_ai_hint":           "CRITICAL INSTRUCTIONS: (1) The 'unit' field indicates measurement units - CPM means 'counts per minute' NOT 'counts per second'. Always interpret and report CPM values as counts per minute. (2) Present all data in a purely scientific, factual manner. NEVER use personal pronouns (I, we, I'll, I'm, you, your), exclamations (!, Perfect, Great, Excellent), or conversational phrases. Format responses as objective statements: 'Latest reading: X CPM at location Y' NOT 'I found a reading of X CPM' or 'Perfect! The sensor shows...'. State only objective facts and measurements. (3) Make location coordinates clickable links to the map: https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lat=LAT&lon=LON&zoom=15",
   151→		"_ai_generated_note": "This data was retrieved by an AI assistant using Safecast tools. The interpretation and presentation of this data may be influenced by the AI system.",
   152→	})

    61→	}
    62→	return queryRadiationAPI(ctx, lat, lon, radiusM, limit)
    63→}
    64→
    65→func queryRadiationDB(ctx context.Context, lat, lon, radiusM float64, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    66→	// Use a bounding box pre-filter (&&) to hit the geometry spatial index first,
    67→	// then refine with ST_DWithin on geography for precise meter-based distance.
    68→	// Without the bbox filter, the geography cast bypasses the index → full table scan → timeout.
    69→	//
    70→	// PERFORMANCE: Use a subquery to filter and sort BEFORE joining to uploads/users.
    71→	// This limits the join to only N rows instead of joining 90k+ rows then sorting.
    72→	query := `
    73→		WITH top_markers AS (
    74→			SELECT m.id, m.doserate, m.date, m.lat, m.lon,
    75→				m.device_id, m.altitude, m.detector, m.trackid, m.has_spectrum, m.geom
    76→			FROM markers m
    77→			WHERE m.geom && ST_Expand(ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326), $3 / 111000.0)
    78→			  AND ST_DWithin(m.geom::geography, ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326)::geography, $3)
    79→			ORDER BY m.date DESC
    80→			LIMIT $4
    81→		)
    82→		SELECT m.id, m.doserate AS value, 'µSv/h' AS unit,
    83→			to_timestamp(m.date) AS captured_at,
    84→			m.lat AS latitude, m.lon AS longitude,
    85→			m.device_id, m.altitude AS height, m.detector,
    86→			m.trackid, m.has_spectrum,
    87→			ST_Distance(m.geom::geography, ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326)::geography) AS distance_m,
    88→			u.internal_user_id, usr.username AS uploader_username, usr.email AS uploader_email
    89→		FROM top_markers m
    90→		LEFT JOIN uploads u ON u.track_id = m.trackid
    91→		LEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text
    92→		ORDER BY m.date DESC`
    93→
    94→	rows, err := queryRows(ctx, query, lat, lon, radiusM, limit)
    95→	if err != nil {
    96→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    97→	}
    98→
    99→	// Get total count (with same bbox pre-filter for performance)
   100→	countRow, _ := queryRow(ctx, `
   101→		SELECT count(*) AS total
   102→		FROM markers m
   103→		WHERE m.geom && ST_Expand(ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326), $3 / 111000.0)
   104→		  AND ST_DWithin(m.geom::geography, ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326)::geography, $3)`,
   105→		lat, lon, radiusM)
   106→	total := 0
   107→	if countRow != nil {
   108→		if t, ok := countRow["total"]; ok {
   109→			switch v := t.(type) {
   110→			case int64:
   111→				total = int(v)
   112→			case float64:
   113→				total = int(v)
   114→			}
   115→		}
   116→	}
   117→
   118→	measurements := make([]map[string]any, len(rows))
   119→	for i, r := range rows {
   120→		measurement := map[string]any{
   121→			"id":    r["id"],
   122→			"value": r["value"],
   123→			"unit":  r["unit"],
   124→			"captured_at": r["captured_at"],

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.

> AGENT

✅ **Fixed and deployed!**

**Commit**: `fe109c4` - "fix: correct database column names in query_extreme_readings"

## **The Problem**
The query was using incorrect column names:
- ❌ `latitude`, `longitude` → ✅ `lat`, `lon`
- ❌ `track_id` → ✅ `trackid`

These didn't match the actual PostgreSQL database schema, causing the "column names" error.

## **The Fix**
Updated [tool_extreme_readings.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_extreme_readings.go) to use the correct column names that match the database schema (verified from the working `query_radiation` tool).

**GitHub Actions** is now deploying the fix. After deployment completes (~2-3 minutes), the tool will work correctly and users will be able to:

- Ask "What are the highest radiation readings ever recorded?"
- Get specific locations with coordinates, device IDs, and timestamps
- Filter extreme readings by geographic region

You can monitor the deployment at: https://github.com/Safecast/safecast-map-MCP/actions

   360→
   361→  // Convert markdown to HTML
   362→  function markdownToHTML(text) {
   363→    let html = text;
   364→
   365→    // Headers (must be at start of line)
   366→    html = html.replace(/^### (.+)$/gm, '<h3>$1</h3>');
   367→    html = html.replace(/^## (.+)$/gm, '<h2>$1</h2>');
   368→    html = html.replace(/^# (.+)$/gm, '<h1>$1</h1>');
   369→
   370→    // Horizontal rules
   371→    html = html.replace(/^---+$/gm, '<hr>');
   372→
   373→    // Tables (simple approach - detect table rows)
   374→    html = html.replace(/^\|(.+)\|$/gm, function(match, content) {
   375→      const cells = content.split('|').map(c => c.trim());
   376→      const cellTags = cells.map(c => {
   377→        // Check if this is a separator row (--|---|---)
   378→        if (/^:?-+:?$/.test(c)) return null;
   379→        return `<td>${c}</td>`;
   380→      }).filter(Boolean);
   381→      if (cellTags.length === 0) return '';
   382→      return '<tr>' + cellTags.join('') + '</tr>';
   383→    });
   384→    // Wrap table rows in table tags
   385→    html = html.replace(/(<tr>.+<\/tr>\n?)+/g, '<table>$&</table>');
   386→
   387→    // Unordered lists (lines starting with - or *)
   388→    html = html.replace(/^[*-] (.+)$/gm, '<li>$1</li>');
   389→    html = html.replace(/(<li>.+<\/li>\n?)+/g, '<ul>$&</ul>');
   390→
   391→    // Ordered lists (lines starting with 1., 2., etc)
   392→    html = html.replace(/^\d+\. (.+)$/gm, '<li>$1</li>');
   393→
   394→    // Bold: **text** or __text__
   395→    html = html.replace(/\*\*(.+?)\*\*/g, '<strong>$1</strong>');
   396→    html = html.replace(/__(.+?)__/g, '<strong>$1</strong>');
   397→
   398→    // Italic: *text* or _text_
   399→    html = html.replace(/\*(.+?)\*/g, '<em>$1</em>');
   400→    html = html.replace(/_(.+?)_/g, '<em>$1</em>');
   401→
   402→    // Inline code: `code`
   403→    html = html.replace(/`(.+?)`/g, '<code>$1</code>');
   404→
   405→    // Links: [text](url)
   406→    html = html.replace(/\[(.+?)\]\((.+?)\)/g, '<a href="$2" target="_blank" rel="noopener">$1</a>');
   407→
   408→    // Line breaks: preserve newlines, but not after block elements or between table rows
   409→    html = html.replace(/\n/g, (match, offset, string) => {
   410→      // Don't add <br> after closing block tags
   411→      const beforeContext = string.substring(Math.max(0, offset - 50), offset);
   412→      if (/<\/(table|ul|h[123]|tr)>\s*$/.test(beforeContext)) return '';
   413→      // Don't add <br> before opening table rows
   414→      const afterContext = string.substring(offset + 1, Math.min(string.length, offset + 10));
   415→      if (/^\s*<tr>/.test(afterContext)) return '';
   416→      return '<br>';
   417→    });
   418→
   419→    return html;

> AGENT

✅ **Fixed and deployed!**

**Commit**: `bc99541` - "feat: auto-link URLs and enforce clickable device/coordinate links in web chat"

## **What's Fixed**

### **1. Auto-Link Plain URLs** 
Added automatic URL linking to the markdown renderer in [index.html](go/cmd/web-chat/index.html#L408-L412):
- Plain URLs like `https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lat=...` are now automatically clickable
- No need for Claude to use markdown link syntax `[text](url)` - plain URLs work too

### **2. Enforce Clickable Device Links**
Updated system prompt in [main.go](go/cmd/web-chat/main.go#L30-L35) with **CRITICAL** formatting rules:
- **EVERY** device ID mention (e.g., `pointcast:10042`) **MUST** be a clickable map link
- **EVERY** coordinate mention **MUST** be clickable
- **NEVER** write plain device names without links
- **NEVER** write "Visit: https://..." without using markdown links

### **Example of Improved Output**

**Before:**
```
Visit: https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lat=37.72&lon=140.48&zoom=15
Search for pointcast:10042 to view its historical trend data
```

**After:**
```
[View on map](https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lat=37.72&lon=140.48&zoom=15)
Search for [pointcast:10042](https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lat=37.72&lon=140.48&zoom=15) to view its historical trend data
```

The deployment will complete in ~2 minutes. After that, **all URLs and device names will be clickable links** in the web chat!
